### S46-08 · feat(api): the organisation's MCP gateway: one setting, a sealed credential, a test through it

**Wave** 5 · **Priority** P0 · **Size** L · **Depends on** `S46-05`, `S46-07` · **Contract change** no

**PR title:** `feat(api): the organisation's MCP gateway: one setting, a sealed credential, a test through it (S46-08)`

**Goal.** An org_admin sets, reads, deletes and tests the organisation's one MCP gateway:
- endpoint and routing;
- auth: bearer | header | oauth_client_credentials | jwt_assertion, plus an optional identity header;
- mode off | available | required;
- timeout.
The credential is sealed and write-only. Every change is audited without the secret, and the store is checked before any write.
Setting the gateway is a governed add: its host and its token endpoint are opened for the api and workers families (409 egress_required, or allow_egress). Private names and http are accepted only for operator-listed hosts.
The service builds the injected client configuration that S46-09 reuses: URL, routing header, and a per-request headers provider with an OAuth token cache and a fresh assertion. It also exposes the gateway's hosts for S46-19.

**Context.**

Template:
- apps/api/src/choregos_api/services/outbound_proxy.py: SETTINGS_KEY L35; SECRET_NAME L37 with `# noqa: S105`; stored() L42; state_of() L50; _config L72; run_test L142; _scrub L136.
- routers/outbound_proxy.py: PUT L52-106 (require_store, first-write secret rule, settings dict copy, audit fields), DELETE L109-136, test L139-167.
- The router file routers/mcp_gateway.py and schemas/mcp_gateway.py exist since S46-07.

Egress (services/egress.py):
- ensure_or_409(session, principal, organisation, targets, allow_egress=, origin=, reason=) L421-453. It returns early unless CHOREGOS_EGRESS_MODE is enforce. allow_egress needs Permission.TOOLS_GRANT, which org_admin has.
- targets_of_urls(urls, family) L240.
- Families: 'api', 'workers' and 'runners' (never 'runners' here). Governed writes run `connector.call` in the workers under egress_scope_for_org(..., 'workers', ...) (effets.py L104-112).
- `egress_scope(session, organisation, family, origin)` L124.
- services/platform_network.py: check_platform_host(session, org_id, url, purpose) L102 (422 when the organisation's bounds refuse the host); host_of L60; with_identity L271 (adds user_agent).

Guarded HTTP: choregos_adapters.http.egress.guarded(transport) L76. The OAuth token POST must go through it, inside the caller's egress scope.

Adapters (S46-03, S46-05):
- McpClient(headers=, headers_provider=), negotiated_version, server, test(), McpError, HeadersProvider.
- build('mcp', 'mcp-gateway', cfg) with the injected keys route, gateway_url, gateway_headers, headers_provider, timeout_s, user_agent, server.
- choregos_adapters.fake_mcp_gateway() returns the process-wide fake (a monkeypatched FAKE_MCP_GATEWAY when set).

Identity (S46-07), services/mcp_gateway_identity.py: CallIdentity, GatewayRefusal, AssertionKeyMissing, ensure_assertion_key, signing_key, mint_assertion, issuer_of, assertion_state.

config.py (hot spot): one new field `mcp_gateway_internal_hosts: list[str] = Field(default_factory=list)` (env CHOREGOS_MCP_GATEWAY_INTERNAL_HOSTS, a JSON list), placed after egress_platform_change (L211) with an English comment. Tests change it with monkeypatch.setenv plus reset_settings_cache.

Tests run under CHOREGOS_FAKES=1 and CHOREGOS_EGRESS_MODE=warn (apps/api/tests/conftest.py L16-19). Enforce-mode tests set the env as apps/api/tests/test_egress_enforced.py does. Module-level caches (_TOKENS, the token transport) leak between tests unless an autouse fixture clears them.

mcp-gateway connectors are OrgConnector rows with type 'mcp-gateway' (db/models.py L613). AuditLog.payload holds audit fields.

**Files.**

- Create: `apps/api/src/choregos_api/services/mcp_gateway.py`, `apps/api/tests/test_mcp_gateway_setting.py`
- Modify: `apps/api/src/choregos_api/routers/mcp_gateway.py`, `apps/api/src/choregos_api/schemas/mcp_gateway.py`, `apps/api/src/choregos_api/schemas/__init__.py`, `apps/api/src/choregos_api/config.py`, `apps/api/tests/test_contract_conformance.py`

**Steps.**

1. schemas/mcp_gateway.py: add McpGatewayAuth, McpGateway, McpGatewayState (with `assertion`), McpGatewayPut (secret: Field(default=None, repr=False), allow_egress), McpGatewayTestRequest and McpGatewayTest, exactly as in the S46-06 contract. Export them.
2. config.py: add mcp_gateway_internal_hosts, as described in context.
3. services/mcp_gateway.py (first line SPDX). Constants:
   - SETTINGS_KEY = 'mcp_gateway';
   - SECRET_NAME = 'org/mcp-gateway/credential' with `# noqa: S105 - a name in the secret store, not a secret`;
   - GATEWAY_TYPE = 'mcp-gateway'; ORIGIN = 'mcp-gateway'; TOKEN_SKEW_S = 30;
   - PRIVATE_SUFFIXES = ('.svc', '.svc.cluster.local', '.cluster.local', '.local', '.internal').
   Define:
   - `@dataclass(frozen=True) class GatewaySetting`: the contract's fields minus secret_set, with `from_stored(dict)`.
   - `def stored(organisation) -> GatewaySetting | None`.
   - `class GatewayNotConfigured(GatewayRefusal)` with code 'gateway_not_configured' and an English message naming the organisation.
4. `def check_endpoint(url, routing, internal_hosts) -> None` raises unprocessable with loc ['body', 'endpoint']:
   - replace '{server}' with 'server' before urlsplit;
   - the scheme is https, or http only when the host is in internal_hosts (case-insensitive exact match);
   - no username or password, query or fragment;
   - an IP literal (ipaddress) is refused unless listed;
   - a dotless name, 'localhost', or a name ending with a PRIVATE_SUFFIXES entry is refused unless listed;
   - routing 'path' requires exactly one '{server}', in the path; routing 'header' forbids '{server}'.
   Apply the same host rule to auth.token_url (loc ['body', 'auth', 'token_url']).
5. `def validate_put(body, previous, secret_known) -> None` answers 422 for:
   - header routing without server_header;
   - type 'header' without header_name;
   - oauth without token_url or client_id;
   - jwt_assertion without audience;
   - identity_header set without audience;
   - jwt_assertion WITH a secret;
   - bearer, header or oauth without a secret when none is stored, or when auth.type differs from the stored type;
   - timeout_s outside 1-120.
6. Hosts and URLs:
   - `def server_url(setting, server) -> str`: path routing replaces '{server}' with urllib.parse.quote(server, safe=''); header routing returns the endpoint.
   - `def egress_targets(setting) -> list[Target]` = targets_of_urls([endpoint with '{server}' -> 'x', token_url], 'api') + the same for 'workers'.
   - `def gateway_host(organisation) -> str | None`: the normalised host of the stored endpoint (platform_network.host_of after replacing '{server}').
   - `def hosts_in_use(setting: GatewaySetting | None) -> set[str]`: the endpoint host and the token_url host. S46-19 uses both.
7. OAuth token cache:
   - module `_TOKENS: dict[str, tuple[str, float]]`; `def clear_token_cache()`.
   - `def set_token_transport(t: httpx.AsyncBaseTransport | None)` for tests. When unset and fakes_enabled(), use choregos_adapters.fake_mcp_gateway().transport().
   - `async def _oauth_token(org_id, setting, client_secret, refresh) -> str`. Cache key: sha256 of the '|'-joined org_id, token_url, client_id, scope, resource, token_audience and sha256(client_secret).
   - On a miss or a refresh, POST token_url with httpx.AsyncClient(transport=guarded(token transport), timeout=setting.timeout_s): form grant_type=client_credentials, scope (when set), resource (default: the endpoint's scheme://host[:port]), audience (when set), HTTP Basic auth.
   - On 200 with access_token: cache it until now + expires_in (default 300) - TOKEN_SKEW_S, and remember_secret it.
   - Otherwise raise McpError(f"the MCP gateway's token endpoint refused the client (HTTP {code})", code).
8. `async def client_config(session, organisation, server, identity) -> dict[str, Any]` (mode is NOT checked here):
   - Read the setting; raise GatewayNotConfigured when it is None.
   - When the type needs it, read the sealed credential with store.get_many and remember_secret it.
   - When auth is jwt_assertion or identity_header is set, load (kid, pem) via signing_key (it raises AssertionKeyMissing).
   - Return {'route': 'gateway', 'gateway_url': server_url(...), 'gateway_headers': {server_header: server} if header routing else {}, 'headers_provider': provider, 'timeout_s': setting.timeout_s}.
   The provider is an async closure `(refresh: bool) -> dict[str, str]` that never touches the session. It returns:
   - bearer: {'Authorization': 'Bearer ' + secret};
   - header: {header_name: secret};
   - oauth: {'Authorization': 'Bearer ' + await _oauth_token(...)};
   - jwt_assertion: {'Authorization': 'Bearer ' + mint_assertion(...)};
   - plus {identity_header: mint_assertion(...)} when identity_header is set and the type is not jwt_assertion.
9. More service functions:
   - `async def client_for_server(session, organisation, server, identity) -> Any` = build('mcp', GATEWAY_TYPE, await with_identity(session, organisation.id, {'server': server, **await client_config(...)})).
   - `async def state_of(session, organisation, store=None) -> McpGatewayState`: secret_set via store.meta; assertion via assertion_state.
10. routers/mcp_gateway.py, GET /orgs/{org}/mcp-gateway (getMcpGateway): return state_of.
11. PUT /orgs/{org}/mcp-gateway (putMcpGateway), in this order, all checks before any write:
   - (a) _admin_organisation;
   - (b) check_endpoint and validate_put;
   - (c) store = require_store({'credential': body.secret} if body.secret else None); and when auth.type == 'jwt_assertion' or identity_header is set, also require_store({'assertion_key': 'x'}) (409 NO_KEY_MESSAGE without a secrets key);
   - (d) check_platform_host for the endpoint and token_url (purpose "the organisation's MCP gateway");
   - (e) ensure_or_409(session, principal, organisation, egress_targets(setting), allow_egress=body.allow_egress, origin=ORIGIN, reason="the organisation's MCP gateway");
   - (f) when a secret is given, remember_secret and put_values(session, store, org.id, 'org/mcp-gateway', {'credential': secret}, principal.email); for jwt_assertion, delete any stored credential;
   - (g) ensure_assertion_key when needed;
   - (h) write settings[SETTINGS_KEY] = {'gateway': {...}, 'updated_by', 'updated_at'} on a copy of the settings dict, keeping the other keys;
   - (i) clear_token_cache();
   - (j) audit 'mcp_gateway.update' with mode, endpoint, routing, server_header, auth_type, header_name, token_url, client_id, audience, identity_header, timeout_s, secret_changed, and direct_mcp_connectors (names of the type 'mcp' connectors when mode is required). Never the secret.
   - Return state_of.
12. DELETE /orgs/{org}/mcp-gateway (deleteMcpGateway):
   - When OrgConnector rows of type 'mcp-gateway' exist, raise conflict("the MCP gateway of {org} still serves connectors: delete them first", extra={'code': 'gateway_in_use', 'connectors': sorted(names)}).
   - Otherwise pop the key, delete SECRET_NAME, clear_token_cache, audit 'mcp_gateway.delete', and return 204.
   - It is idempotent, and assertion keys are kept.
13. POST /orgs/{org}/mcp-gateway/test (testMcpGateway), body McpGatewayTestRequest:
   - 404 when there is no setting; mode is ignored, as the outbound-proxy test ignores `enabled`.
   - identity CallIdentity(org=slug, subject=f'person:{principal.email}', purpose='test', server=body.server).
   - Inside `async with egress_scope(session, organisation, 'api', 'mcp-gateway:test')`, run asyncio.wait_for(client.test(), setting.timeout_s).
   - Answer ok, protocol_version=client.negotiated_version, server_info=client.server.get('serverInfo'), tools, elapsed_ms.
   - McpError, httpx.HTTPError, TimeoutError and GatewayRefusal give ok False, with error = redact(str(exc)) and the credential and any token scrubbed.
   - Audit 'mcp_gateway.test' with server and ok.
14. test_contract_conformance.py: remove the four remaining S46 entries and the two remaining paths (CONTRACT_ONLY and CONTRACT_ONLY_PATHS end empty).
15. Write the tests:
   - An autouse fixture: set_secret_store(FakeSecretStore()), clear_token_cache(), set_token_transport(None); restore all three after the test.
   - Monkeypatch choregos_adapters.FAKE_MCP_GATEWAY with a FakeMcpGateway whose upstream 'supplier' is a FakeMcpServer built through a `_server()` helper.
   Run the commands. PR with the template; STATUS row: proves the setting, its validation, its store and egress gates, and a test through the fake gateway in every auth mode; does not prove that runs or governed writes use it (S46-09), the runner guard (S46-19) or a real gateway (S46-16).

### S46-09 · feat(api): the broker, governed writes and discovery reach mcp-gateway connectors through the gateway; required mode

**Wave** 10 · **Priority** P0 · **Size** L · **Depends on** `S46-08`, `S46-18`, `S45-04` · **Contract change** no

**PR title:** `feat(api): the broker, governed writes and discovery reach mcp-gateway connectors through the gateway; required mode (S46-09)`

**Goal.** An `mcp-gateway` connector works like an `mcp` connector everywhere a connector is called: a run's read, a run's write, an approval-gated call, an approved governed write in the workers, and discovery. It goes through the organisation's gateway, with the signed identity of the call.
Mode `required` refuses to declare direct `mcp` connectors and to call existing ones, for reads, writes and approvals alike. `off`, or no gateway, refuses gateway connectors. A lost assertion key is refused the same way.
Every such refusal happens before anything leaves:
- a 403 tool error of type network_policy with its code, or a 403 problem for an approval-gated call;
- a 409 at declaration and discovery;
- a final EffetRefuse in the workers.
It never creates an Action row or a cost-ledger line, never returns a 500 and never triggers a Temporal retry.

**Context.**

Three places build an org connector client today, each with `build(kind, type, await with_identity(session, org_id, await resolved_configuration(...)))`:
- apps/api/src/choregos_api/services/broker.py `_call` (the former courtier._appeler L382-440):
  - enforce_connector_call L399-409 maps refusals to 403 {'type': 'network_policy'} and 429 {'type': 'rate_limited'};
  - the build is at L410-418;
  - errors map as McpError -> 502, AdapterError -> 422, SecretIntrouvable/httpx -> 502.
- apps/api/src/choregos_api/effets.py `_appeler_le_connecteur` L124-213:
  - `await _within_the_network_bounds(...)` L175 (defined L115-121);
  - config and build L176-189;
  - except chain L198-213 (UpstreamError, ErreurMcp, EgressRefusedError, httpx.HTTPError, AdapterError -> EffetRefuse, which is permanent).
  It runs in the orchestrator workers under egress family 'workers' (L104-112).
- apps/api/src/choregos_api/routers/org_connectors.py `discover_operations` L522-618:
  - imports ErreurMcp and empreinte_du_schema at L532;
  - enforce_connector_call L539-542;
  - config and build L543-552;
  - `async with egress_scope(..., 'api', ...)` L555;
  - `except (ErreurMcp, SecretIntrouvable, httpx.HTTPError)` L557, which marks the connector 'error' and commits.

The write paths that never reach `_call`:
- broker.py `write_through_action` (former ecrire_par_une_action L243-312) creates the Action, approves it by policy and starts the workflow. internal.py L946-953 then counts the operation's price when `nouvelle` is True.
- broker.py `propose_call` (former proposer_l_appel L162-174) creates a pending Action. internal.py L944 hard-codes code 202.
So required/off would only be caught in the workers, AFTER an Action and a price exist, unless both functions check the route first.
- internal.py skips the ledger for result type in {'network_policy', 'rate_limited', 'query_redacted', 'url_redacted'} (L963-968).
- agent_sessions.py does the same for network_policy and rate_limited (L533).
- An ApiError raised inside propose_call propagates as an HTTP 403 problem, and the request's transaction rolls back.

Declaring is the one door: `declare_org_connector` L188-283 (check_type_config L205, check_connector_declaration L216, egress_gate_for_connector L217). The catalogue install also goes through it.

services/platform_network.py `enforce_connector_call` L190-220 reads the host from `connector_host(instance.config)` and returns early when None. An mcp-gateway config has no URL, so add a `host` keyword. Per-host politeness (`calls_to_host`) is skipped for gateway connectors (polite=False): upstream rate limits are the gateway's job (ADR 0097 §4).

Identity sources:
- Run (db/models.py L227-276): id, work_item_id, agent_slug L247, agent_version L248, initiated_by L275.
- WorkItem: tracker_key L186, initiated_by L205.
- Action: run_id, work_item_id, and decisions entries {'by', 'decision'}. A policy approval writes by 'policy' (services/actions.py L241-247); a person's decision appends an entry (L370-377).

From S46-07 and S46-08:
- services/mcp_gateway_identity.py: CallIdentity, GatewayRefusal (ConfigurationError with `code`), AssertionKeyMissing.
- services/mcp_gateway.py: stored, client_config, GatewayNotConfigured, GATEWAY_TYPE, host_of usage.

Test patterns:
- apps/api/tests/test_courtier.py L15-83: OUTILS, the fournisseur fixture, _preparer, run token, envelope answers {status_code, result}.
- test_platform_network.py: _ceiling L20-22 (PUT the org policy ceiling) and _decisions L25.
- The effect is invoked directly in apps/api/tests/test_action_de_transition.py L180: `effet('connector.call')(ContexteEffet(session, action, projet), appel)`.
The gateway tests cannot reuse test_courtier._preparer, which declares a direct connector.

Cross-track: effets.py, org_connectors.py and platform_network.py are renamed by the S45 apps/api story. Schedule it after S46-10, or rebase it.

**Files.**

- Create: `apps/api/tests/test_mcp_gateway_routing.py`
- Modify: `apps/api/src/choregos_api/services/mcp_gateway.py`, `apps/api/src/choregos_api/services/mcp_gateway_identity.py`, `apps/api/src/choregos_api/services/broker.py`, `apps/api/src/choregos_api/effets.py`, `apps/api/src/choregos_api/routers/org_connectors.py`, `apps/api/src/choregos_api/services/platform_network.py`

**Steps.**

1. services/mcp_gateway.py:
   - Add `class GatewayRequired(GatewayRefusal)` with code 'gateway_required', and `@dataclass(frozen=True) class Route` (host: str | None, via_gateway: bool).
   - Add `async def route_of(session, instance) -> Route`:
     - organisation = await session.get(Organization, instance.org_id); setting = stored(organisation);
     - instance.type == GATEWAY_TYPE: raise GatewayNotConfigured("`{name}` goes through the MCP gateway of {org}, which has none (or it is off): an administrator sets it on the Administration page") when the setting is None or mode == 'off'; otherwise return Route(gateway_host(organisation), True);
     - instance.kind == 'mcp' and instance.type == 'mcp' and setting and setting.mode == 'required': raise GatewayRequired("{org} reaches its MCP servers only through its MCP gateway: the direct connector `{name}` is not called; declare it as an mcp-gateway connector");
     - otherwise return Route(connector_host(instance.config), False).
2. `async def connector_client(session, instance, identity) -> Any`:
   - await route_of(...), which raises;
   - config = await with_identity(session, instance.org_id, await resolved_configuration(session, instance.org_id, instance.kind, instance.type, instance.config, instance.secret_refs));
   - for GATEWAY_TYPE, injected = await client_config(session, organisation, str(config['server']), identity), and config = {**config, **injected, 'timeout_s': float(instance.config['timeout_s']) if instance.config.get('timeout_s') else injected['timeout_s']} (the connector's timeout wins);
   - return build(instance.kind, instance.type, config).
3. `async def check_declaration(session, organisation, kind, type_name) -> None`:
   - kind 'mcp', type 'mcp', mode required: conflict(..., extra={'code': 'gateway_required'});
   - type GATEWAY_TYPE with no setting or mode off: conflict(..., extra={'code': 'gateway_not_configured'}).
4. services/mcp_gateway_identity.py:
   - `async def identity_of_run(session, run, project, connector, tool) -> CallIdentity`:
     - org slug from Organization;
     - when run is None (agent sessions): subject f'project:{project.slug}', or f'org:{slug}' without a project;
     - otherwise subject f'run:{run.id}', run=run.id, work_item = the WorkItem's tracker_key, agent = f'{run.agent_slug}@{run.agent_version}' when agent_slug is set, initiator = run.initiated_by;
     - connector = connector.name, server = connector.config.get('server'), tool = tool, purpose 'tool_call'.
   - `async def identity_of_action(session, action, project, connector, tool) -> CallIdentity`: subject f'action:{action.id}', run=action.run_id, work item key and initiator from the WorkItem, action=action.id, approved_by = tuple(d['by'] for d in action.decisions or [] if d.get('decision') == 'approve'), purpose 'action'.
   - `def identity_of_person(org_slug, email, connector, purpose) -> CallIdentity`.
5. services/platform_network.py `enforce_connector_call`: add the keyword `host: str | None = None` and use `host = host or connector_host(instance.config)`. Nothing else changes.
6. broker.py `_call`:
   - Switch the client error import to `from choregos_adapters.mcp import McpError`.
   - Before enforce_connector_call: `try: route = await route_of(session, instance) except GatewayRefusal as refused: return 403, {'error': str(refused), 'type': 'network_policy', 'code': refused.code}`.
   - Call enforce_connector_call(..., host=route.host, polite=not route.via_gateway).
   - Replace the config and build block with `client = await connector_client(session, instance, await identity_of_run(session, run, project, instance, outil.operation.name))`.
   - In the except chain, add `except GatewayRefusal as refused:` returning the same 403 network_policy body, placed BEFORE `except AdapterError`.
   - Keep the McpError, AdapterError and httpx mapping.
7. broker.py write paths:
   - At the top of `write_through_action`: `try: await route_of(session, tool.connector) except GatewayRefusal as refused: return 403, {'error': str(refused), 'type': 'network_policy', 'code': refused.code}, False`. No Action is created; internal.py prices 0 because the third value is False, and skips the ledger for network_policy.
   - At the top of `propose_call`: on GatewayRefusal raise ApiError(403, 'Network policy', str(refused), type_='network_policy', extra={'code': refused.code}) before `_propose_tool_action`.
   - Add English comments citing ADR 0097.
8. effets.py:
   - `_within_the_network_bounds(ctx, instance, label)`: call `route = await route_of(ctx.session, instance)`; on GatewayRefusal, raise EffetRefuse(f'{label}: {exc}') from exc. Then call enforce_connector_call(ctx.session, instance, project=ctx.project, polite=False, host=route.host).
   - In `_appeler_le_connecteur`, replace L176-189 (config and build) with `client = await connector_client(ctx.session, instance, await identity_of_action(ctx.session, ctx.action, ctx.project, instance, operation))`. GatewayRefusal derives from AdapterError, so the existing `except AdapterError` turns it into a permanent EffetRefuse.
   - Write the added comments in English.
9. routers/org_connectors.py:
   - declare_org_connector: `await check_declaration(session, organisation, kind, type_name)` right after check_type_config (L205).
   - discover_operations:
     - Replace L539-552: route = await route_of(...) BEFORE the try (GatewayRefusal -> conflict(str(e), extra={'code': e.code})).
     - Then enforce_connector_call(session, instance, polite=False, host=route.host).
     - Inside the try: `lister = getattr(await connector_client(session, instance, identity_of_person(organisation.slug, principal.email, instance, 'discovery')), 'list_tools', None)`.
     - Add `except GatewayRefusal as refused: raise conflict(str(refused), extra={'code': refused.code}) from refused` as the FIRST except clause, so a lost key never marks the connector 'error'.
     - Switch ErreurMcp and empreinte_du_schema to McpError and schema_digest on the lines you change.
10. Write apps/api/tests/test_mcp_gateway_routing.py:
   - A helper `_prepare_gateway(client, project, monkeypatch, *, auth='jwt_assertion')`:
     - set_secret_store(FakeSecretStore());
     - PUT the gateway (endpoint https://gw.example.test/{server}/mcp, audience 'gw');
     - GET jwks.json;
     - monkeypatch choregos_adapters.FAKE_MCP_GATEWAY = FakeMcpGateway(auth='jwt_assertion', jwks=..., audience='gw', issuer=..., upstreams={'supplier': GatewayUpstream(server=_server(OUTILS, token='upstream-key'), token='upstream-key')});
     - declare 'supplier' {type 'mcp-gateway', config {server 'supplier'}}; discover;
     - open suivi_commande allowed, commander_poste allowed, stock approval;
     - agent 'coordinateur' with mcp_servers [{connector 'supplier', tools ['*']}];
     - WorkItem 'varga/x#1' and Run 'run-gw-1' (agent_slug coordinateur, agent_version 1, initiated_by 'person:admin@varga.dev');
     - mint_run_token.
   - A helper `_prepare_direct(...)` for a direct 'fournisseur' connector, as test_courtier does, with FAKE_MCP.
   Run the commands. PR with the template; STATUS row: proves reads, writes, approvals, governed effects and discovery through the fake gateway with a signed identity, both refusal modes and the lost key; does not prove a real gateway (S46-16), the runner guard (S46-19) or the console (S46-13, S46-14).

### S46-10 · feat(api): MCP catalogue entries install through the organisation's gateway

**Wave** 11 · **Priority** P1 · **Size** M · **Depends on** `S46-09`, `S46-14` · **Contract change** no

**PR title:** `feat(api): MCP catalogue entries install through the organisation's gateway (S46-10)`

**Goal.** Each catalogue entry says how this organisation can install it (`install_routes`). Any entry can be installed through the gateway when the organisation has one, stdio (needs_hosting) and OAuth (needs_oauth) entries included. Such an install creates an `mcp-gateway` connector with no credential in Choregos and shows the gateway procedure. `install` keeps saying what Choregos can do alone.

**Context.**

apps/api/src/choregos_api/routers/mcp_catalogue.py:
- `_dto` L65-101: `procedure=procedure_of_mcp_entry(...)` L87; `can.install = can_install and entry.install == 'installable'` L93-94; `cannot_reason = install_reason or refusal` L98.
- list_mcp_catalogue L124-166: the `install` query param L133 stays.
- _refusal_for L169-181.
- install_mcp_catalogue_entry L184-233: it refuses needs_oauth/needs_hosting via _refusal_for at L200, builds OrgConnectorCreate(type='mcp', kind='mcp', config={'url', 'auth_header'?}, secrets) and calls declare_org_connector(..., catalogue_entry=f'{id}@{version}').

choregos_core.mcp_catalogue.install_kind (packages/core/src/choregos_core/mcp_catalogue/__init__.py L97) stays unchanged.

apps/api/src/choregos_api/schemas/catalogues.py:
- McpCatalogueEntryDto L49-78 (install Literal L69, can, cannot_reason, procedure L78);
- McpCatalogueInstall L86-96 (name pattern ^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{1,62}$, secret_refs, secret_values, auth_header, availability, allow_egress).

From S46-08 and S46-09, services/mcp_gateway.py: stored(organisation) -> GatewaySetting | None (with .mode), GATEWAY_TYPE. check_declaration runs inside declare_org_connector: 409 gateway_required for a direct mcp connector in required mode, and 409 gateway_not_configured for a gateway connector without a gateway.

Contract (S46-06): install_routes is an optional array [direct, gateway]; McpCatalogueInstall.route is nullable, and gateway_server has the pattern ^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,127}$.

The procedure connector.mcp.mcp-gateway exists since S46-05. Existing tests are in apps/api/tests/test_mcp_catalogue.py; the catalogue is the shipped file, so pick entries by their `install` value from the list answer, never by a hard-coded id.

**Files.**

- Create: `apps/api/tests/test_mcp_catalogue_gateway.py`
- Modify: `apps/api/src/choregos_api/routers/mcp_catalogue.py`, `apps/api/src/choregos_api/schemas/catalogues.py`

**Steps.**

1. schemas/catalogues.py:
   - McpCatalogueEntryDto: add `install_routes: list[Literal['direct', 'gateway']] = Field(default_factory=list)`.
   - McpCatalogueInstall: add `route: Literal['direct', 'gateway'] | None = None` and `gateway_server: str | None = Field(default=None, pattern=r'^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,127}$')`.
2. mcp_catalogue.py: `def install_routes(entry, gateway_mode: str | None) -> list[str]`:
   - 'direct' when entry.install == 'installable' and gateway_mode != 'required';
   - 'gateway' when gateway_mode in ('available', 'required');
   - in that order.
3. list_mcp_catalogue: read `mode = (setting.mode if (setting := stored(organisation)) else None)` once and pass `routes=install_routes(e, mode)` into _dto. _dto takes a new keyword `routes: list[str]` and sets exactly:
   - `body['install_routes'] = routes`;
   - `can.install = can_install and bool(routes)`;
   - `body['cannot_reason'] = install_reason or (None if 'gateway' in routes else _refusal_for(entry))`;
   - `body['procedure'] = 'connector.mcp.mcp-gateway'` when routes == ['gateway'], else today's procedure.
4. install_mcp_catalogue_entry:
   - Resolve route = body.route, or by default 'gateway' when mode == 'required' or (entry.install != 'installable' and mode == 'available'), else 'direct'.
   - route 'gateway':
     - Answer 422 when secret_refs, secret_values or auth_header are given: "through the gateway the server's credential is the gateway's: Choregos stores none".
     - declaration = OrgConnectorCreate(name=body.name or entry.id, type=GATEWAY_TYPE, kind='mcp', config={'server': body.gateway_server or entry.id}, secret_refs={}, secret_values={}, availability=body.availability, allow_egress=body.allow_egress).
     - declare_org_connector(..., catalogue_entry=...). check_declaration gives 409 gateway_not_configured without a gateway.
   - route 'direct': today's path unchanged, _refusal_for included. check_declaration gives 409 gateway_required in required mode.
5. Write the tests (FakeSecretStore and the gateway PUT as in S46-08's tests). Run the commands. PR with the template; STATUS row: proves the routes and both installs against the shipped catalogue; does not prove the console (S46-14).

**Recalibrated on main 3156d12 (overrides the text above).**

- routers/mcp_catalogue.py has not changed since b3a7893, but the plan's line numbers were two lines off from the start. The correct anchors are: `_dto` L67-101 (`procedure=procedure_of_mcp_entry(...)` L89, `install=can_install and entry.install == "installable"` L95, `body["cannot_reason"] = install_reason or refusal` L99); list_mcp_catalogue decorator L126, `install` query param L133, return L166; `_refusal_for` L169-181; install_mcp_catalogue_entry decorator L184, the `_refusal_for` refusal L200, `OrgConnectorCreate(type="mcp", ...)` L217-226, `declare_org_connector(...)` L227-233. schemas/catalogues.py anchors are unchanged (McpCatalogueEntryDto L49-78, McpCatalogueInstall L86-96).
- Plugin servers (ADR 0096) are organisation connectors of kind/type 'mcp', created by services/plugin_servers.provision and not through declare_org_connector. Their `catalogue_entry` is 'plugin:<plugin>/<server>@<version>'. `_installed()` (L104) keys them as 'plugin:<plugin>/<server>', which matches no catalogue id: leave it, and add no plugin-server logic to the catalogue routes. A gateway install whose name equals a plugin server's connector name gets the existing 409 'a connector `x` already exists' from declare_org_connector. Add no special case for it.

**Tests (each fails without the change).**

- apps/api/tests/test_mcp_catalogue_gateway.py :: test_without_a_gateway_routes_are_today_s :: an installable entry has install_routes ['direct']; a needs_hosting entry has [], can.install False and its hosting refusal
- apps/api/tests/test_mcp_catalogue_gateway.py :: test_with_a_gateway_every_entry_installs_through_it :: after PUT mode available, installable -> ['direct', 'gateway']; needs_hosting and needs_oauth -> ['gateway'] with can.install True for the admin and cannot_reason null; `install` values unchanged
- apps/api/tests/test_mcp_catalogue_gateway.py :: test_a_gateway_only_entry_shows_the_gateway_procedure :: with mode available, a needs_hosting entry has procedure 'connector.mcp.mcp-gateway'; an installable entry keeps its own procedure
- apps/api/tests/test_mcp_catalogue_gateway.py :: test_required_mode_leaves_only_the_gateway :: mode required -> every entry ['gateway']
- apps/api/tests/test_mcp_catalogue_gateway.py :: test_a_gateway_install_creates_an_mcp_gateway_connector_without_credential :: installing a needs_hosting entry with {} -> 201; type 'mcp-gateway', config == {'server': <entry id>}, secret_refs empty or null, catalogue_entry '<id>@<version>'; installing another with gateway_server 'gh-prod' and name 'gh-prod' -> config server 'gh-prod'
- apps/api/tests/test_mcp_catalogue_gateway.py :: test_a_gateway_install_with_a_secret_is_refused :: route gateway with secret_values {'token': 'x'} -> 422; nothing is created
- apps/api/tests/test_mcp_catalogue_gateway.py :: test_a_direct_install_in_required_mode_is_409 :: route direct for an installable entry -> 409 with code gateway_required
- apps/api/tests/test_mcp_catalogue_gateway.py :: test_a_gateway_install_without_gateway_is_409 :: no gateway, route gateway -> 409 with code gateway_not_configured
- apps/api/tests/test_mcp_catalogue.py (existing) :: all :: pass unchanged

**Acceptance.**

- [ ] choregos_core.mcp_catalogue is unchanged.
- [ ] A gateway install stores no credential and no URL.
- [ ] make ci is green.
- [ ] The PR body STATUS row says what it proves and what it does not.

**Commands.**

```bash
uv run pytest -q apps/api/tests/test_mcp_catalogue_gateway.py apps/api/tests/test_mcp_catalogue.py
make ci
```

**Do not.**

- Do not change install_kind, INSTALL_ORDER, the catalogue file or its schema.
- Do not let a gateway install carry secret_refs, secret_values or auth_header.
- Do not change the `install` query filter of the list route.
- Do not touch routers/org_connectors.py or services/mcp_gateway.py.
- Never edit docs/plan/STATUS.md or docs/plan/08-backlog.yaml.

### S46-11 · test(e2e): an agent's read and governed writes reach a server through the MCP gateway

**Wave** 11 · **Priority** P0 · **Size** M · **Depends on** `S46-09` · **Contract change** no

**PR title:** `test(e2e): an agent's read and governed writes reach a server through the MCP gateway (S46-11)`

**Goal.** This is the end-to-end proof, with the real Temporal test server and the platform's workers (in memory). An organisation's gateway in JWT assertion mode, required mode, fronts a fake supplier server:
- A run reads through the gateway.
- An allowed write runs as a governed action in the workers, through the gateway, once even when replayed.
- An approval-gated write waits for a person and carries that person's name.
Each request carries an identity verifiable against the JWKS. The upstream credential exists only in the gateway, and the run token never leaves.

**Context.**

Model: tests/e2e/test_ecriture_gouvernee.py:
- _preparer L33-87: a FakeMcpServer with gestes, the FAKE_MCP monkeypatch (after S46-01), connector declaration, discovery, opening operations, an agent with mcp_servers, a Run row, mint_run_token.
- The `worker` fixture runs the workers.
- The replay check is at L104-113 (the same call returns the same action id, and the server is called once).

tests/e2e/conftest.py: the `environnement_e2e` fixture sets CHOREGOS_FAKES=1, so egress mode is warn. It provides Platform (client, project_id, project_slug) and login. The API and the workers share one process, so the monkeypatched choregos_adapters.FAKE_MCP_GATEWAY and the set_secret_store store apply to both.

Approval by another person: apps/api/tests/test_boite_des_decisions.py L55-71. The agent's owner is refused (separation of duties); a member 'rh@varga.dev' with role project_owner approves via POST /api/v1/projects/{id}/actions/{action_id}/decision {'decision': 'approve'}.

An allowed write is approved by the policy: decisions [{'by': 'policy', 'decision': 'approve'}] (services/actions.py L241-247).

From S46-04 to S46-09:
- choregos_adapters.FAKE_MCP_GATEWAY;
- FakeMcpGateway(auth='jwt_assertion', jwks=..., audience=..., issuer=..., upstreams={'supplier': GatewayUpstream(server=..., token='upstream-key')});
- GET /api/v1/orgs/varga/mcp-gateway/jwks.json and PUT /api/v1/orgs/varga/mcp-gateway;
- the connector type mcp-gateway.

FakeMcpServer French fields (outils, jeton, gestes, appels, recues) go only in `_server()` and `_received()`/`_called()` helpers at the top of the file, so the S45 rename touches three lines.

tests/e2e is excluded from `make test` (pyproject norecursedirs). It runs with `make e2e` / `-m e2e` (the CI e2e job).

**Files.**

- Create: `tests/e2e/test_mcp_gateway.py`

**Steps.**

1. `_preparer(platform, monkeypatch)`:
   - set_secret_store(FakeSecretStore()), restored to None in a fixture finaliser; log in as admin;
   - PUT /orgs/varga/mcp-gateway {mode 'required', endpoint 'https://gw.example.test/{server}/mcp', routing 'path', auth {type 'jwt_assertion'}, audience 'choregos-e2e', allow_egress true};
   - read the state's assertion.issuer, and GET jwks.json;
   - monkeypatch choregos_adapters.FAKE_MCP_GATEWAY = FakeMcpGateway(auth='jwt_assertion', jwks=jwks, audience='choregos-e2e', issuer=issuer, upstreams={'supplier': GatewayUpstream(server=_server([order_pc write, order_status read with readOnlyHint, order_monitor write], token='upstream-key', gestures={'order_pc': ..., 'order_monitor': ...}), token='upstream-key')});
   - declare the connector {name 'supplier', type 'mcp-gateway', config {server 'supplier'}}; discover;
   - open order_status allowed, order_pc allowed, order_monitor approval;
   - agent 'buyer' with mcp_servers [{connector 'supplier', tools ['*']}];
   - WorkItem 'varga/billing-api#11' with initiated_by 'person:admin@varga.dev';
   - Run 'run-gw-1' (agent_slug buyer, agent_version 1, initiated_by 'person:admin@varga.dev');
   - a run token.
2. Write the four tests below; each write test runs inside `async with worker():`.
3. Run `uv run pytest -q tests/e2e/test_mcp_gateway.py -m e2e`, the whole e2e package, and `make ci`. PR with the template; STATUS row: proves the governed path end to end against the fake gateway with real Temporal workers; does not prove a real gateway or a real cluster network.

**Recalibrated on main 3156d12 (overrides the text above).**

- On main the client of an organisation connector is built by `services/plugin_servers.connector_client(session, instance)`, no longer by `choregos_adapters.build`. It is called from services/courtier.py `_appeler` (~L408), effets.py `_appeler_le_connecteur` (~L176) and routers/org_connectors.py discover_operations (~L562). S46-09 makes mcp-gateway connectors go through it. This e2e must exercise those real paths: do not monkeypatch connector_client, use_plugin_transport or use_plugin_runtime.
- test_nothing_of_choregos_s_reaches_the_upstream_and_no_upstream_key_is_in_choregos: 'the org has no direct mcp connector' means no OrgConnector row with `type == 'mcp'` AND `plugin_server IS NULL` (rule b: plugin servers are type 'mcp' too). Query it with `OrgConnector.plugin_server.is_(None)`, the idiom of plugin_servers.connectors_of L274.

**Tests (each fails without the change).**

- tests/e2e/test_mcp_gateway.py :: test_a_read_goes_through_the_gateway_with_the_runs_identity :: envelope status_code 200; the gateway's last call claims: run 'run-gw-1', work_item 'varga/billing-api#11', agent 'buyer@1', initiator 'person:admin@varga.dev', tool 'order_status', iss == the state's issuer
- tests/e2e/test_mcp_gateway.py :: test_an_allowed_write_is_one_governed_action_through_the_gateway :: inside the worker, the call returns 200 with the upstream's structuredContent; the upstream recorded exactly one order_pc; replaying the same call returns the same action id, still with one upstream call; claims purpose 'action' and approved_by ['policy']
- tests/e2e/test_mcp_gateway.py :: test_an_approval_gated_write_waits_for_a_person_and_carries_their_name :: the call -> 202 pending_approval, upstream untouched; rh@varga.dev approves; under the worker the action succeeds; exactly one upstream order_monitor; claims approved_by ['rh@varga.dev'] and action == the action id
- tests/e2e/test_mcp_gateway.py :: test_nothing_of_choregos_s_reaches_the_upstream_and_no_upstream_key_is_in_choregos :: the upstream saw only 'Bearer upstream-key'; no gateway or upstream request contains the run token; no FakeSecretStore value is 'upstream-key'; the org has no direct mcp connector

### S46-12 · feat(charts): global.mcpGateway opens the API and the workers to the MCP gateway, never the runners

**Wave** 6 · **Priority** P1 · **Size** M · **Depends on** `S46-08` · **Contract change** no

**PR title:** `feat(charts): global.mcpGateway opens the API and the workers to the MCP gateway, never the runners (S46-12)`

**Goal.** With the default NetworkPolicies on, a deployment can reach its MCP gateway. The new values block `global.mcpGateway`:
- renders one NetworkPolicy from the API and orchestrator pods to the gateway (an in-cluster namespace and labels, or CIDRs, on its ports);
- passes CHOREGOS_MCP_GATEWAY_INTERNAL_HOSTS to the platform;
- appends the gateway host names to the api and workers egress ceiling when those families are declared.
Runner pods are never selected, and a default render changes nothing.

**Context.**

charts/choregos/templates/networkpolicies.yaml (150 lines, everything inside `if .Values.networkPolicies.enabled`):
- `choregos-default-deny-egress` L4-12.
- `choregos-allow-platform` L13-51: platform namespaces only, no Internet; it selects every pod except choregos-runner, choregos-egress and choregos-buildkitd.
- `choregos-runner-egress` L58-84, which must stay unchanged.
Pod labels: app.kubernetes.io/name=choregos-api and choregos-orchestrator (DEFAULT_SELECTORS, apps/api/src/choregos_api/services/egress.py L51-55). Approved MCP writes run in the orchestrator workers (effets.py L104-112), so both API and orchestrator pods need the opening.

charts/choregos/templates/_helpers.tpl `choregos.commonEnv` L91:
- The `$poses` list of chart-set names L92 is checked against global.extraEnv duplicates L97-106. OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT is appended to it only when set (L94-96): follow that pattern.
- `$egress` L115; CHOREGOS_EGRESS_CEILING is rendered from `$egress.ceiling` at L118-121.
- commonEnv is included by charts/choregos/charts/api/templates/deployment.yaml L200, by orchestrator/templates/deployments.yaml L60, and by the migrations job and the trace-drift cronjob (harmless).

charts/choregos/values.yaml: the global.egress block L200-245 (ceiling L215); networkPolicies.enabled L572-573.

The API reads CHOREGOS_MCP_GATEWAY_INTERNAL_HOSTS (the config.py field from S46-08, a JSON list).

Do NOT add the gateway hosts to global.egress.denyDomains / CHOREGOS_NETWORK_INSTANCE_DENY: that list also bounds the platform's own calls (services/platform_network.py bounds_of). Runs are kept off the gateway by S46-19.

Render-and-read tests: tests/charts/test_egress_values.py (helpers _render with --set-json and _env(docs, deployment) L22-37; skipped without helm, and CI has helm). charts-lint is part of make ci.

**Files.**

- Create: `tests/charts/test_mcp_gateway_chart.py`
- Modify: `charts/choregos/values.yaml`, `charts/choregos/templates/networkpolicies.yaml`, `charts/choregos/templates/_helpers.tpl`, `docs/deployment.md`

**Steps.**

1. values.yaml: under `global`, after the `egress` block, add `mcpGateway`, with English comments citing ADR 0097 ('another product, never part of this chart'):
   - internalHosts: [];
   - hosts: [];
   - namespace: '';
   - podLabels: {};
   - cidrs: [];
   - ports: [443].
2. _helpers.tpl, in choregos.commonEnv:
   - Define `{{- $gw := .Values.global.mcpGateway | default dict }}` next to `$egress`.
   - After the OTEL block at L94-96, append "CHOREGOS_MCP_GATEWAY_INTERNAL_HOSTS" to `$poses` ONLY when `$gw.internalHosts` is non-empty.
   - Render `- name: CHOREGOS_MCP_GATEWAY_INTERNAL_HOSTS` / `value: {{ toJson $gw.internalHosts | quote }}` only when the list is non-empty.
   - Replace L118-121 with:
     - `{{- $ceiling := deepCopy ($egress.ceiling | default dict) }}`;
     - for each family in (list "api" "workers"), when `hasKey $ceiling $family`, set it to `uniq (concat (get $ceiling $family) ($gw.hosts | default list))`;
     - render CHOREGOS_EGRESS_CEILING from $ceiling when non-empty.
   - Never add a family that was not declared, and never touch 'runners'.
3. networkpolicies.yaml, inside `if .Values.networkPolicies.enabled`, after choregos-allow-platform:
   - Define `{{- $gw := .Values.global.mcpGateway | default dict }}`.
   - When `$gw.namespace` or `$gw.cidrs` is set, render the NetworkPolicy `choregos-mcp-gateway-egress`:
     - podSelector matchExpressions app.kubernetes.io/name In [choregos-api, choregos-orchestrator];
     - policyTypes [Egress];
     - one egress rule whose `to` holds a namespaceSelector on kubernetes.io/metadata.name (with a podSelector matchLabels from podLabels when given) and/or one ipBlock per CIDR;
     - `ports` from $gw.ports with protocol TCP.
4. docs/deployment.md: add a section 'An MCP gateway (ADR 0097)' near 'Network access: the egress allowlist and its ceiling' (L514). Cover:
   - the values;
   - why the API and the workers;
   - why never the runners (and that the API also denies the host to runs);
   - the internal hosts rule;
   - why the gateway is not in denyDomains;
   - one example for an in-cluster gateway and one for an external one.
5. Write the tests (helm template plus PyYAML, as test_egress_values.py does). Run the commands. PR with the template; STATUS row: proves the rendered objects; does not prove that a CNI enforces them on a real cluster.

**Recalibrated on main 3156d12 (overrides the text above).**

- charts/choregos/templates/networkpolicies.yaml is now 155 lines. `choregos-allow-platform` spans L13-56: at L47-51 it gained an opening to the plugin servers namespace (`global.plugins.servers.namespace.name`) when `global.plugins.servers.enabled` (ADR 0096). Leave it unchanged. The `---` after it is L57 and `choregos-runner-egress` is L58-89 (same content as before). Render `choregos-mcp-gateway-egress` right after L56.
- charts/choregos/values.yaml: the `global.egress` block is still L204-245 (ceiling L215, denyDomains L240); insert `global.mcpGateway` after L245, before `logLevel`. `networkPolicies.enabled` is now L612-613. A new `global.plugins` block (sources, defaultPlugins, offline, servers; ~L340-379) exists: do not touch it.
- charts/choregos/charts/api/templates/deployment.yaml now includes choregos.commonEnv at L229 (not L200). The orchestrator include (deployments.yaml L60) and every _helpers.tpl anchor (commonEnv L91, `$poses` L92, OTEL L94-96, `$egress` L115, CEILING L118-121) are unchanged.
- Plugin servers run in their own namespace, behind a NetworkPolicy the API renders (ADR 0096). They are never selected by the gateway policy. Extend test_an_in_cluster_gateway_opens_the_api_and_the_workers_only with a render that also sets `global.plugins.servers.enabled=true`. Assert that the gateway policy's selector values are still exactly ['choregos-api', 'choregos-orchestrator'], and that choregos-allow-platform equals the same render without global.mcpGateway.
- docs/deployment.md: 'Network access: the egress allowlist and its ceiling' is now at L519 (not L514), and the new sections 'Plugins' (L632) and 'Plugin servers' (L688) exist. In the new MCP gateway section, add one sentence: a plugin's server is run by the platform in-cluster, is never reached through the MCP gateway and needs nothing from global.mcpGateway. Link it to #plugin-servers.
- Commands: add tests/charts/test_plugin_servers_chart.py and tests/charts/test_plugins_values.py to the pytest line, because they render the same templates.
- ADR number: the values comment and the docs section title cite ADR 0097 (the gateway ADR, renumbered from 0088). There is no 0088 file on main, and 0088 is held by an open PR.

**Tests (each fails without the change).**

- tests/charts/test_mcp_gateway_chart.py :: test_nothing_by_default :: a default render has no NetworkPolicy named choregos-mcp-gateway-egress, no CHOREGOS_MCP_GATEWAY_INTERNAL_HOSTS on the api and orchestrator deployments, and no CHOREGOS_EGRESS_CEILING (as today)
- tests/charts/test_mcp_gateway_chart.py :: test_an_in_cluster_gateway_opens_the_api_and_the_workers_only :: global.mcpGateway={namespace: 'mcp', podLabels: {app: 'gw'}, ports: [8080]} renders the policy, whose selector values == ['choregos-api', 'choregos-orchestrator'], with namespace mcp, pod label app=gw and port 8080; the choregos-runner-egress policy equals the default render's
- tests/charts/test_mcp_gateway_chart.py :: test_an_external_gateway_is_opened_by_cidr :: cidrs ['203.0.113.0/24'] -> an ipBlock cidr 203.0.113.0/24 on port 443
- tests/charts/test_mcp_gateway_chart.py :: test_internal_hosts_reach_the_api_and_the_workers :: internalHosts ['gw.mcp.svc'] -> CHOREGOS_MCP_GATEWAY_INTERNAL_HOSTS == '["gw.mcp.svc"]' in the api and orchestrator env
- tests/charts/test_mcp_gateway_chart.py :: test_the_ceiling_gains_the_gateway_only_for_declared_families :: egress.ceiling={api: ['api.github.com'], runners: ['github.com']} and mcpGateway.hosts ['gw.example.test'] -> json.loads(CHOREGOS_EGRESS_CEILING) == {'api': ['api.github.com', 'gw.example.test'], 'runners': ['github.com']} (no workers key added, runners untouched)
- tests/charts/test_mcp_gateway_chart.py :: test_extra_env_cannot_set_the_variable_twice :: with mcpGateway.internalHosts ['gw.mcp.svc'] and global.extraEnv [{name: CHOREGOS_MCP_GATEWAY_INTERNAL_HOSTS, value: x}], the render fails; without internalHosts, the same extraEnv renders
- tests/charts/test_egress_values.py (existing) :: all :: pass unchanged

**Acceptance.**

- [ ] make charts-lint is green (helm lint plus kubeconform).
- [ ] A default render has no choregos-mcp-gateway-egress policy, no CHOREGOS_MCP_GATEWAY_INTERNAL_HOSTS, and the same CHOREGOS_EGRESS_CEILING as before (tested).
- [ ] The runner policy is unchanged; docs/deployment.md is updated.
- [ ] The PR body STATUS row says what it proves and what it does not.

**Commands.**

```bash
uv run pytest -q tests/charts/test_mcp_gateway_chart.py tests/charts/test_egress_values.py tests/charts/test_faux_de_demo.py
make charts-lint
make ci
```

**Do not.**

- Do not select choregos-runner, choregos-egress or choregos-buildkitd pods.
- Do not deploy any gateway workload from the chart.
- Do not add the gateway to global.egress.denyDomains.
- Do not name a gateway vendor in values or templates (boundary test).
- Do not change global.egress semantics for undeclared families.
- Do not edit charts/choregos/charts/* subcharts: both deployments already include choregos.commonEnv.
- Never edit docs/plan/STATUS.md or docs/plan/08-backlog.yaml.

### S46-13 · feat(web): the MCP gateway card on the Administration page

**Wave** 3 · **Priority** P1 · **Size** M · **Depends on** `S46-06` · **Contract change** no

**PR title:** `feat(web): the MCP gateway card on the Administration page (S46-13)`

**Goal.** An org_admin sets, tests and removes the organisation's MCP gateway from Administration, and creates or rotates the assertion key. The card lets them:
- pick the mode, with a warning listing the direct MCP connectors that `required` stops;
- set the endpoint, with routing validation;
- set the auth type and its fields, with a write-only secret;
- set the audience and the identity header;
- see the issuer and the JWKS URL to give the gateway.
PUT handles 409 egress_required through the existing egress modal, and a refused removal names the connectors. It works against mocks and the real API alike.

**Context.**

apps/web/AGENTS.md: this Next.js has breaking changes. Read the relevant guide in apps/web/node_modules/next/dist/docs/ before writing code.

Template: apps/web/src/components/admin/outbound-proxy.tsx (OutboundProxyCard, 200 lines): useQuery keyed ['outbound-proxy', org], enabled when canEdit; a write-only password field; a test button; GesteConfirme (imported from @/components/geste-confirme L6) for delete.
- Helpers: apps/web/src/lib/outbound-proxy.ts.
- Test: apps/web/tests/outbound-proxy.test.tsx.
- Mounted at apps/web/src/app/admin/page.tsx L16 (import) and L149 `<OutboundProxyCard org={org} canEdit={orgAdmin} />`.

API client: apps/web/src/lib/api.ts L822-827 (outboundProxy functions); types are re-exported from @choregos/contracts in apps/web/src/lib/types.ts. Mocks: apps/web/src/mocks/data.ts L2517-2553 (mockOutboundProxy, outboundProxyApi) and the dispatch at L2655. These three files are hot spots, and S46-14 edits data.ts after this story.

Egress modal: `useEgressGuard` and `egressFlag(allow)` from apps/web/src/components/egress/egress-provider.tsx (L33, L38), used in apps/web/src/app/admin/connectors/page.tsx L251-262.

Generated types after S46-06: McpGateway, McpGatewayAuth, McpGatewayState, McpGatewayPut, McpGatewayTest, McpGatewayTestRequest, McpGatewayAssertion, McpGatewayAssertionKey, McpGatewayConflict.

The console never shows a story id (see the PROXY_KINDS note in lib/outbound-proxy.ts). UI text is English (ADR 0039 and 0046).

tests/licences/test_entetes_spdx.py requires `// SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0` in the first 5 lines of every file under apps/web/src.

**Files.**

- Create: `apps/web/src/components/admin/mcp-gateway.tsx`, `apps/web/src/lib/mcp-gateway.ts`, `apps/web/tests/mcp-gateway.test.tsx`
- Modify: `apps/web/src/app/admin/page.tsx`, `apps/web/src/lib/api.ts`, `apps/web/src/lib/types.ts`, `apps/web/src/mocks/data.ts`, `docs/usage.md`

**Steps.**

1. types.ts: re-export the nine generated types.
2. api.ts, after the outbound proxy functions, add:
   - mcpGateway(org) GET;
   - putMcpGateway(org, body: McpGatewayPut) PUT;
   - deleteMcpGateway(org) DELETE;
   - testMcpGateway(org, body: McpGatewayTestRequest) POST /test;
   - rotateMcpGatewayAssertionKey(org) POST /assertion-key.
3. lib/mcp-gateway.ts (first line SPDX):
   - `endpointProblem(endpoint, routing)`: https or http URL; no '@', '?' or '#'; path routing needs exactly one {server} in the path; header routing allows none.
   - `AUTH_TYPES`: value, label, needsSecret.
   - `MODES`: off | available | required, each with a one-line explanation.
   - `SERVER_PATTERN`.
4. components/admin/mcp-gateway.tsx (first line SPDX): `McpGatewayCard({org, canEdit})`, mirroring OutboundProxyCard:
   - a form keyed by updated_at;
   - fields: mode, endpoint, routing plus server_header, auth type with its own fields (header: header_name; oauth: token_url, client_id, scope, resource, token_audience), audience (required for jwt_assertion), identity_header, timeout_s;
   - a secret password field: empty keeps the stored one, with a 'set' hint from secret_set;
   - when mode is required, list the direct `mcp` connectors from the existing org connectors query and warn that they will not be called;
   - Save: `guard((allow) => api.putMcpGateway(org, {...body, ...egressFlag(allow)}), ...)`;
   - Test: a server-name input, showing ok, version, server name, tool count and error;
   - the assertion block: issuer and JWKS URL with copy buttons, the key list, and Rotate key (GesteConfirme);
   - Remove (GesteConfirme): a 409 with code gateway_in_use shows the `connectors` names.
   Return null when !canEdit.
5. admin/page.tsx: import and render `<McpGatewayCard org={org} canEdit={orgAdmin} />` right after OutboundProxyCard.
6. mocks/data.ts:
   - `mockMcpGateway: McpGatewayState`, with an assertion block showing an example issuer and JWKS URL.
   - `mcpGatewayApi(method, bare, body)` for the five routes:
     - PUT validates like the API (a first write needs a secret unless jwt; jwt creates a mock key);
     - test returns ok with tools 3;
     - DELETE returns 409 {code: 'gateway_in_use', connectors} when a mock connector of type mcp-gateway exists.
   - Dispatch it next to outboundProxyApi (L2655).
7. docs/usage.md:
   - Add 'MCP gateway' to the Administration summary near L94.
   - After the outbound proxy section near L1865, add a paragraph: what it is, the modes, the auth types, what to give the gateway (the issuer and JWKS URL), and ADR 0097.
8. Write the tests and run make web-ci. PR with the template; STATUS row: proves the card against mocks; does not prove it against the real API (S46-08) or in a browser e2e.

**Recalibrated on main 3156d12 (overrides the text above).**

- apps/web/src/lib/api.ts: the outbound proxy functions are now at L829-834 (not L822-827); add the five gateway functions after L834. admin/page.tsx (import L16, card L149), data.ts (mockOutboundProxy L2517-2553, dispatch L2655) and egress-provider.tsx (L33, L38) are unchanged.
- Rule (b), required mode exempts plugin servers. The required-mode warning must not list a plugin's server (ADR 0096). Plugin servers are OrgConnector rows of kind/type 'mcp'. OrgConnectorDto has no plugin_server field, but the installer always writes `catalogue_entry` = 'plugin:<plugin>/<server>@<version>' and `created_by` = 'plugin:<plugin>' (services/plugin_servers.py provision). Export `isPluginServer(c: OrgConnector): boolean` from lib/mcp-gateway.ts as `(c.catalogue_entry ?? '').startsWith('plugin:')`. Warn only about connectors with `c.type === 'mcp' && !isPluginServer(c)`. If S46-06 added an explicit plugin-server field to OrgConnector, use that field instead. The console paragraph '**Outbound proxy.**' is now at L1911 (not ~L1865): add the MCP gateway paragraph after it. Cite ADR 0097 (renumbered from 0088).

- apps/web/tests/mcp-gateway.test.tsx :: saves a bearer gateway and never shows the secret again :: filling endpoint, mode and secret, then Save, calls putMcpGateway with the secret; after the refetch the secret field is empty and shows 'set'

- apps/web/tests/mcp-gateway.test.tsx :: warns before required mode stops direct MCP connectors :: with a mock direct mcp connector 'fournisseur', selecting required shows its name
- apps/web/tests/mcp-gateway.test.tsx :: reports a test result :: Test with server 'supplier' shows 'ok' and '3 tools'
- apps/web/tests/mcp-gateway.test.tsx :: names the connectors when removal is refused :: a mocked 409 gateway_in_use shows the connector names

### S46-15 · feat(dev): a fake MCP gateway in the local install, behind --profile mcp-gateway

**Wave** 13 · **Priority** P2 · **Size** S · **Depends on** `S46-09`, `S46-04` · **Contract change** no

**PR title:** `feat(dev): a fake MCP gateway in the local install, behind --profile mcp-gateway (S46-15)`

**Goal.** `docker compose --profile mcp-gateway up` adds the demo fakes server and the fake MCP gateway in front of it. An administrator can then set the gateway at http://mcp-gateway:8091/{server}/mcp, declare `mcp-gateway` connectors, discover them and call them. The default `docker compose up` is unchanged.

**Context.**

compose.yaml:
- x-platform-env L16-31, shared by api, worker, seed and migrate. The org is CHOREGOS_BOOTSTRAP_ORG demo, and CHOREGOS_PUBLIC_URL is http://localhost:${CHOREGOS_PORT_WEB:-3000}.
- x-platform-image L39-44 (choregos-api:local, built from docker/api.Dockerfile, which contains choregos_adapters and uvicorn).
- Profile precedents: temporal-ui L95-102 and ecphoria (memory) L250-262.
- network default L267-269.

The demo fakes server: `python -m choregos_adapters.fakes.serveur` (port env CHOREGOS_DEMO_PORT, default 8090; token CONNECTOR_DEMO_JETON) serves /{mdm,shipping,access_control,supplier,fournisseur,cd}/mcp.

The fake gateway (S46-04): `python -m choregos_adapters.fakes.mcp_gateway`, env CHOREGOS_FAKE_GATEWAY_* (port 8091).

The API accepts http and a dotless host only when listed in CHOREGOS_MCP_GATEWAY_INTERNAL_HOSTS (S46-08). A dotless name is an internal host for the organisation's network bounds (choregos_core.network.is_internal).

In compose the issuer is the public URL (http://localhost:3000/...), which the gateway container cannot fetch, so jwt_assertion needs an explicit JWKS URL on the internal network: http://api:8000/api/v1/orgs/demo/mcp-gateway/jwks.json.

tests/dev/test_local_install.py reads compose.yaml with helpers _services (L18) and _env (L22). test_the_platform_and_its_dependencies_are_all_there checks a subset; test_no_platform_process_runs_with_global_fakes (L31) checks api, worker, seed and migrate; test_every_image_is_built_here_or_pinned (L63) needs `build` or a pinned image. tests/ci/test_local_install_job.py covers the CI job. .github/workflows/ci.yml is a hot spot and is not touched.

**Files.**

- Create: `tests/dev/test_local_mcp_gateway.py`
- Modify: `compose.yaml`, `docs/development.md`

**Steps.**

1. compose.yaml: add to x-platform-env `CHOREGOS_MCP_GATEWAY_INTERNAL_HOSTS: '["mcp-gateway"]'`, with an English comment saying it is harmless without the profile.
2. Add the service `demo-fakes`: profiles [mcp-gateway], `<<: *platform-image`, command ['python', '-m', 'choregos_adapters.fakes.serveur'], environment {CHOREGOS_DEMO_PORT: '8090', CONNECTOR_DEMO_JETON: local-upstream-only}. No ports.
3. Add the service `mcp-gateway`: profiles [mcp-gateway], `<<: *platform-image`, command ['python', '-m', 'choregos_adapters.fakes.mcp_gateway'], depends_on [demo-fakes]. No ports. Environment:
   - CHOREGOS_FAKE_GATEWAY_PORT '8091';
   - CHOREGOS_FAKE_GATEWAY_AUTH bearer;
   - CHOREGOS_FAKE_GATEWAY_TOKEN local-gateway-only;
   - CHOREGOS_FAKE_GATEWAY_UPSTREAMS: a JSON object mapping supplier, mdm, shipping and access_control to {url: 'http://demo-fakes:8090/<name>/mcp', token: 'local-upstream-only'}.
4. docs/development.md: add a section 'An MCP gateway in the local install (ADR 0097)' with:
   - the command;
   - the console steps: Administration, MCP gateway (endpoint http://mcp-gateway:8091/{server}/mcp, bearer local-gateway-only, allow the host); then Connectors (type "MCP server through the organisation's MCP gateway", server supplier, Discover);
   - the fact that the upstream token never enters Choregos;
   - one line for jwt_assertion: set CHOREGOS_FAKE_GATEWAY_AUTH=jwt_assertion, CHOREGOS_FAKE_GATEWAY_JWKS_URL=http://api:8000/api/v1/orgs/demo/mcp-gateway/jwks.json and CHOREGOS_FAKE_GATEWAY_ISSUER=http://localhost:3000/api/v1/orgs/demo/mcp-gateway (the issuer stays the public URL).
5. Write the tests and run the commands. If Docker is available, run `docker compose --profile mcp-gateway config` to validate. PR with the template; STATUS row: proves the compose file's shape; does not prove a laptop run (a person runs it; the local-install CI job does not start the profile).

**Integrator amendments (they override the text above).**

- Re-anchor every path and symbol of this story to the English names from the rename tables in `docs/upgrade-notes/0.21/` before starting (the story text was written against the names of 2026-10-27).

**Recalibrated on main 3156d12 (overrides the text above).**

- In docs/development.md, the jwt_assertion line must copy the issuer exactly as S46-07 merged it (services/mcp_gateway_identity.py). If S46-07 was changed to keep the issuer stable across organisation renames (ADR 0090), use that format instead of `http://localhost:3000/api/v1/orgs/demo/mcp-gateway`. The JWKS URL `http://api:8000/api/v1/orgs/demo/mcp-gateway/jwks.json` stays valid either way. The section title cites ADR 0097 (renumbered from 0088). compose.yaml anchors (x-platform-env L16, x-platform-image L39, temporal-ui L95-102, ecphoria L250-262, networks L267-269) and the demo fakes server (`choregos_adapters.fakes.serveur`, CHOREGOS_DEMO_PORT, CONNECTOR_DEMO_JETON) are unchanged on main.

### S46-16 · test(live): a real MCP gateway in the nightly, failing when it is set and unreachable

**Wave** 2 · **Priority** P1 · **Size** S · **Depends on** `S46-03`, `S46-04` · **Contract change** no

**PR title:** `test(live): a real MCP gateway in the nightly, failing when it is set and unreachable (S46-16)`

**Goal.** When the nightly has a gateway's secrets, the platform's MCP client is proven against it: initialize, the agreed version, tools/list. An operator gets the same probe as a tool. A gateway that is set but unreachable turns the nightly red; only an unset URL skips. A run without the secrets collects and skips, never errors.

**Context.**

tests/live/conftest.py: require(*names) skips when any variable is missing.

tests/live and tests/e2e are in pytest norecursedirs (pyproject.toml L144-152), so `make test` never collects them. addopts uses `--import-mode=importlib` (L153), and `tools` is not a package (no tools/__init__.py). Only tests/tools/conftest.py puts the repository root on sys.path. A module-level `from tools.mcp_gateway_probe import probe` in tests/live therefore fails at COLLECTION, turning the nightly red even without secrets.

The nightly `live` job (.github/workflows/nightly.yml L112-131) passes secrets as env and runs `uv run pytest tests/live -q -m live -rs`.

McpClient wraps its transport in guarded(): with no egress policy in context and mode enforce, the call is refused with 'no_policy_in_context'. Wrap the probe in choregos_core.egress.use_policy(EgressPolicy(org='live', rules=(AllowedRule(host, (port,), ('api',)),), mode='enforce'), 'api', 'live:mcp-gateway'); see packages/core/src/choregos_core/egress.py L89 and L192-285.

CLIENT_VERSIONS and negotiated_version come from S46-03. FakeMcpGateway and GatewayUpstream come from S46-04.

tools/ is linted and typed (mypy strict); tests/ is linted but not typed. ruff E402 applies to tests, so do the sys.path insertion with plain statements and import inside the test function (PLC0415 is ignored repo-wide).

**Files.**

- Create: `tools/mcp_gateway_probe.py`, `tests/tools/test_mcp_gateway_probe.py`, `tests/live/test_mcp_gateway_live.py`
- Modify: `.github/workflows/nightly.yml`

**Steps.**

1. tools/mcp_gateway_probe.py, first line SPDX:
   - `async def probe(endpoint: str, *, server: str, token: str, routing: str = 'path', server_header: str = 'X-MCP-Server', auth_header: str = 'Authorization', transport: httpx.AsyncBaseTransport | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]`:
     - build the URL (replace {server} for path routing) and the routing headers;
     - inside a use_policy that allows only that host and port, run McpClient(url, headers=..., token=token, auth_header=auth_header, transport=transport).list_tools();
     - return {'version': client.negotiated_version, 'server': client.server.get('serverInfo'), 'tools': len(tools)};
     - NO try/except: errors propagate.
   - `def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int`: a CLI with --server, --token-env (the variable holding the token), --routing, --server-header and --auth-header. It prints JSON and returns exit code 1 on an exception, without printing the token.
2. tests/live/test_mcp_gateway_live.py, pytestmark live:
   - At module level, plain statements: ROOT = pathlib.Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]; insert str(ROOT) on sys.path when absent.
   - In the test:
     - url = os.environ.get('CHOREGOS_LIVE_MCP_GATEWAY_URL', ''); `if not url: pytest.skip(...)`;
     - otherwise ASSERT that CHOREGOS_LIVE_MCP_GATEWAY_SERVER and CHOREGOS_LIVE_MCP_GATEWAY_TOKEN are set (fail, not skip);
     - `from tools.mcp_gateway_probe import probe` inside the function;
     - run probe, with routing, server header and auth header from CHOREGOS_LIVE_MCP_GATEWAY_ROUTING, _SERVER_HEADER and _AUTH_HEADER;
     - assert version in CLIENT_VERSIONS and tools >= 1.
   - The docstring says how to run it.
3. nightly.yml `live` job env: add CHOREGOS_LIVE_MCP_GATEWAY_URL, _SERVER, _TOKEN, _ROUTING, _SERVER_HEADER and _AUTH_HEADER from secrets.
4. Write tests/tools/test_mcp_gateway_probe.py, then run the commands, including the live run with an empty URL, which must report a skip and no collection error. PR with the template; STATUS row: proves the probe against the fake and the failure mode; does not prove a real gateway until the secrets exist (say so).

**Tests (each fails without the change).**

- tests/tools/test_mcp_gateway_probe.py :: test_the_probe_lists_tools_through_a_gateway :: probe('https://gw.test/{server}/mcp', server='s', token='t', transport=FakeMcpGateway(auth='bearer', token='t', upstreams={'s': GatewayUpstream(server=<a FakeMcpServer with tools>)}).transport()) returns tools >= 1 and a version in CLIENT_VERSIONS
- tests/tools/test_mcp_gateway_probe.py :: test_an_unreachable_gateway_raises_and_does_not_skip :: probe('http://127.0.0.1:9/{server}/mcp', server='s', token='t') raises httpx.HTTPError or McpError, never pytest.skip.Exception
- tests/tools/test_mcp_gateway_probe.py :: test_a_wrong_token_is_an_error :: a FakeMcpGateway with token 'right' and probe token 'wrong' raises McpError with code 401
- tests/tools/test_mcp_gateway_probe.py :: test_the_cli_never_prints_the_token :: main(['https://gw.test/{server}/mcp', '--server', 's', '--token-env', 'X']) with X='secret-value' and an unreachable host returns 1, and capsys output has no 'secret-value'
- tests/live/test_mcp_gateway_live.py :: test_a_real_gateway_lists_a_servers_tools :: (nightly) passes against the configured gateway, fails if it is set and unreachable, skips when the URL is unset

**Acceptance.**

- [ ] `make test` runs the probe tests (tests/tools is collected).
- [ ] `CHOREGOS_LIVE_MCP_GATEWAY_URL= uv run pytest tests/live -q -m live -rs` reports the new test as skipped, with no collection error.
- [ ] The nightly env lists the new secrets.
- [ ] make ci is green.
- [ ] The PR body STATUS row says what it proves and what it does not.

**Commands.**

```bash
uv run pytest -q tests/tools/test_mcp_gateway_probe.py
CHOREGOS_LIVE_MCP_GATEWAY_URL= uv run pytest tests/live -q -m live -rs
make ci
```

**Do not.**

- Do not catch exceptions in probe or in the live test.
- Do not skip when the URL is set.
- Do not import `tools.*` at module level in tests/live.
- Do not print the token.
- Do not edit .github/workflows/ci.yml.
- Never edit docs/plan/STATUS.md or docs/plan/08-backlog.yaml.

**Docs.** `Docstrings of the probe tool and the live test`

### S46-17 · docs: consuming an MCP gateway in integrations and security

**Wave** 14 · **Priority** P1 · **Size** S · **Depends on** `S46-10`, `S46-11`, `S46-12`, `S46-14`, `S46-15`, `S46-16`, `S46-19` · **Contract change** no

**PR title:** `docs: consuming an MCP gateway in integrations and security (S46-17)`

**Goal.** The reference documentation says how an organisation uses its MCP gateway end to end, and what that guarantees:
- the integration steps;
- the security model: trust boundaries, the assertion's claims and the personal data they carry, what the gateway sees, the runner guarantee, rotation, threats and mitigations;
- cross-links between usage, deployment, development, the procedure and ADR 0097.

**Context.**

docs/integrations.md sections: the door L12, Rovo MCP connector L274, a remote agent L309, Limits L392, 'When it does not connect' L399.

docs/security.md is the reference security page. Its broker row (L31) was pointed at services/broker.py by S46-18.

Already written by earlier stories:
- docs/usage.md (S46-13, S46-14);
- docs/deployment.md (S46-12);
- docs/development.md (S46-15);
- docs/procedures/connector.mcp.mcp-gateway.md (S46-05);
- docs/adr/0097 (S46-02).

Facts to state, each pointing at its module:
- Assertion (services/mcp_gateway_identity.py):
  - claims org, project, run, work_item, agent, initiator, action, approved_by, connector, server, tool, purpose;
  - exp - iat = 60 s; ES256; kid; unique jti;
  - issuer {public_url}/api/v1/orgs/{org}/mcp-gateway; JWKS at /jwks.json, public, Cache-Control 300 s, the same 404 for unknown and keyless organisations;
  - rotation keeps the previous key, and a gateway may refuse new assertions until it refetches.
- Egress for api and workers only, and the internal hosts rule (services/mcp_gateway.py).
- The gateway host denied to runs and refused for runners rules; the gateway's own rules (origin mcp-gateway) revoked when it moves or is deleted, while merged rules keep their families (services/egress.py, services/network.py, S46-19).
- The modes and the codes gateway_required, gateway_not_configured, gateway_in_use, gateway_assertion_key_missing: none creates an action or a ledger line (services/broker.py).
- The ledger provider mcp:<connector>.
- The token cache is per process (API, workers).

tests/docs/test_links.py checks links. docs/ may name gateway vendors, but only as examples.

**Files.**

- Modify: `docs/integrations.md`, `docs/security.md`, `docs/README.md`

**Steps.**

1. docs/integrations.md: add a section 'Your organisation's MCP gateway (ADR 0097)' before 'Limits'. Cover:
   - when to use it (servers that need hosting or OAuth, upstream credentials held centrally);
   - what Choregos needs from the gateway: Streamable HTTP, path or header routing, one of the four auth types, and accepting the JWT assertion against the JWKS URL;
   - the steps: Administration, then Connectors or Catalogue;
   - what changes and what does not for agents, policies, approvals and cost.
   Link to the procedure and to usage, deployment and development.
2. docs/security.md: add a section 'MCP gateway' with:
   - the trust boundaries: two policy points;
   - the credentials: the upstream credential is held by the gateway only; the gateway credential is sealed;
   - the assertion: claims, lifetime, personal data (initiator and approver emails), rotation;
   - what the gateway sees: the arguments and results of the calls routed to it;
   - threats and mitigations:
     - a tenant aiming the platform at internals: the internal hosts rule;
     - a compromised gateway: results are still bounded and injection-guarded, and writes still need approval;
     - replay: short exp and jti;
     - agents never reaching it: no runner egress, the API's denial, the chart policy.
   Also add one row to the table, pointing at services/mcp_gateway.py, services/mcp_gateway_identity.py and tests/licences/test_mcp_gateway_boundary.py.
3. docs/README.md: add links to the new sections if it indexes sections.
4. Run the commands. PR with the template; STATUS row: proves the docs are linked and current; does not prove the behaviour (that is the earlier stories).

**Recalibrated on main 3156d12 (overrides the text above).**

- docs/security.md: the broker row ('a tool server flooding or steering the agent...') is now at L33, not L31, because rows for SkillSpector (ADR 0095, L18) and kernel confinement (ADR 0094, L30) were added. S46-18 points that row at services/broker.py. Add the MCP gateway row next to it. docs/integrations.md anchors are unchanged ('Limits' L392, 'When it does not connect' L399).
- Rules (a) and (b): in both the integrations and the security section, state that a plugin's server (ADR 0096: an organisation connector with `plugin_server` set, run by the platform in-cluster in the plugins namespace; services/plugin_servers.py, choregos_adapters.plugin_servers) is not an upstream. It is never reached through the gateway and is not refused by `required` mode, and the boundary test tests/licences/test_mcp_gateway_boundary.py whitelists those two modules. Choregos still never routes, aggregates or re-serves third-party MCP servers. Link docs/plugins.md#servers and docs/deployment.md#plugin-servers; do not duplicate them.
- Organisation renames (ADR 0090): the JWKS URL under a former slug keeps resolving, because OrgAliasMiddleware (org_alias_middleware.py) rewrites `/api/v1/orgs/<alias>/...` and adds a `Link: rel="canonical"` header. Read the merged services/mcp_gateway_identity.py before writing about the issuer. If the issuer carries the slug, say that a rename changes it and the gateway must be given the new issuer. If S46-07 made the issuer stable, say so.
- The gateway ADR is docs/adr/0097-*.md (renumbered from 0088; there is no 0088 file on main). Every link and section title uses 0097, otherwise tests/docs/test_links.py fails.
- docs/README.md indexes pages, not sections (main added a 'Writing a plugin' row). Step 3 is therefore expected to change nothing; leave README.md untouched unless a page is added.
- Vocabulary (ADR 0093 Decision 1): in new text, 'plugin' means the ADR 0093 package and entry-point packages are 'extensions'. Do not call an entry-point extension a plugin.

- tests/docs/test_links.py (existing) :: all links resolve, including the new anchors and ADR 0097
- tests/langue (existing) and tools/check_english.py :: the added lines are English

### S46-18 · refactor(api): the broker lives in services/broker.py in English, services/courtier.py re-exports it

**Wave** 0 · **Priority** P0 · **Size** M · **Depends on** — · **Contract change** no

**PR title:** `refactor(api): the broker lives in services/broker.py in English, services/courtier.py re-exports it (S46-18)`

**Goal.** The broker that S46-09 extends is in English. The whole implementation moves to services/broker.py under English names, and services/courtier.py becomes a re-export shim, so every importer keeps working unchanged. Governed-write action ids, which are persisted, stay byte-identical.

**Context.**

Split from the former S46-01 for size. It shares no file with S46-01 or S46-02, so it runs in W1.

apps/api/src/choregos_api/services/courtier.py (635 lines; docstrings French; strings returned to agents already English).

Module-level imports L18-49:
- asyncio, json, time, uuid, fnmatchcase, sqlalchemy select;
- ..db.models;
- ..secret_store.resolved_configuration;
- .catalogue_rights (SELECTED_PROJECTS, enabled_ids);
- .connecteurs (human_floor, plus_stricte);
- .platform_network (PlatformNetworkRefused, PlatformRateLimited, WHERE, enforce_connector_call, with_identity...).

Definitions:
- SEPARATEUR L51.
- _ESPACE_DES_APPELS L54 = uuid.UUID('6b3f0f3e-6c0e-4f0a-9a52-0e3c8f1d2b41').
- OutilDuCourtier L58 (fields nom, connecteur, operation, sous_validation; property ecriture; method annonce).
- _selection L84, selection_of_spec L107, outils_du_courtier L116, tools_of_selection L120.
- proposer_l_appel L162, _proposer_l_action_de_l_outil L177.
- identifiant_de_l_appel L237: uuid5(namespace, f'{run_id}:{outil}:{canonique}'), where canonique = json.dumps(arguments, sort_keys=True, ensure_ascii=False, default=str).
- ecrire_par_une_action L243, _en_attente L315, _resultat_de_l_action L324.
- ArgumentsRefuses L341 (noqa N818), verifier_les_arguments L345, appeler L361, _appeler L382 (imports ErreurMcp L393).
- TRUNCATED L444, output_refusal L447, result_limit L474, bounded_result L483, bounded_payload L506, _cut L520, _masked_strings L538, _masked L551, _json L578.
- texte_du_resultat L584, session_text L590, _serialized_in_text L601, readable_text L612.

Importers that must keep working unchanged:
- routers/internal.py L822, 877, 925, 938, 960, 1079, 1094, 1111. It also reads `.sous_validation`, `.ecriture`, `.connecteur.name`, `.operation`, `.nom` and `.annonce()`.

- services/agent_realtime.py L67.
- apps/api/tests/test_agent_hosted_sessions.py L479 and test_guard_structured_content.py L79.
`git grep 'services.courtier'` shows no monkeypatch of the module.

Eight test modules import fixtures from apps/api/tests/test_courtier.py:
