### S47-26 · feat(api): put the secret KEKs behind a key-wrapper seam (no KMS shipped)

**Wave** 9 · **Priority** P2 · **Size** M · **Depends on** `S47-12`, `S47-13` · **Contract change** no

**PR title:** `feat(api): put the secret KEKs behind a key-wrapper seam (no KMS shipped)`

**Goal.** Connector secret envelopes wrap their data keys through a KeyWrapper protocol. The environment keyring becomes the default implementation, with no behaviour change, and a plugin can supply a KMS or Vault-transit wrapper. An ADR records that core ships no KMS integration.

**Context.**

apps/api/src/choregos_api/secret_store.py:
- L62-79 `Keyring` (keys, active_id); L81-107 `parse_keyring` / `keyring_from_env` (ENV_KEKS = CHOREGOS_SECRETS_KEKS).
- L122-131 `seal` wraps the DEK with `AESGCM(keyring.active)` and the AAD b'dek|' + aad; L134-141 `_open_dek`.
- L192-276 `SealedDbSecretStore(keyring=None)`, with put, get_many and rewrap.
- L167-183 the `SecretStore` protocol; L344-348 `set_secret_store`.

OrgSecret.kek_id is String(32) (db/models.py:673). The chart injects CHOREGOS_SECRETS_KEKS into both the API and the orchestrator. Existing tests: apps/api/tests/test_secret_store.py and test_connector_secrets.py.

Verdict V8 confirmed, but low severity: P2, a seam plus an ADR.

**Files.**

- Create: `apps/api/tests/test_secret_key_wrapper.py`, `docs/adr/0103-secret-keks-sit-behind-a-key-wrapper.md`
- Modify: `apps/api/src/choregos_api/secret_store.py`, `docs/security.md`, `docs/adr/README.md`

**Steps.**

1. secret_store.py: add `class KeyWrapper(Protocol)` with `active_id: str`, `def wrap(self, dek: bytes, aad: bytes) -> tuple[bytes, bytes, str]` (wrapped, nonce, kek_id) and `def unwrap(self, wrapped: bytes, nonce: bytes, aad: bytes, kek_id: str) -> bytes`. Add `class EnvKeyWrapper` holding a Keyring and implementing both with AES-GCM and b'dek|' + aad, so the bytes are identical to today's.
2. `seal(value, wrapper, org_id, name, version)` and `_open_dek(row, wrapper)` use the wrapper, and `unseal` follows. For backward compatibility, also accept a Keyring argument and wrap it into EnvKeyWrapper.
3. `SealedDbSecretStore.__init__(self, keyring: Keyring | None = None, wrapper: KeyWrapper | None = None)`. `_wrapper()` returns the given wrapper, else EnvKeyWrapper(keyring or keyring_from_env()), else None. `available`, `put`, `get_many` and `rewrap` use it; rewrap selects the rows whose kek_id differs from `wrapper.active_id`.
4. docs/security.md '## Secrets' (L233): the wrapper seam, the default env keyring, and that a KMS/Vault-transit wrapper comes from a plugin (set_secret_store, or a SealedDbSecretStore built with a wrapper); kek_id is at most 32 chars.
5. Write docs/adr/0103-secret-keks-sit-behind-a-key-wrapper.md (accepted, today): context, decision, and the rejected option (shipping AWS KMS/Vault clients in community). Append the README row. Rewrite in English any French comment line you touch in secret_store.py.

**Recalibrated on main 3156d12 (overrides the text above).**

- ADR number: under the plan's renumbering this hardening ADR is 0103 (was 0099). Create docs/adr/0103-secret-keks-sit-behind-a-key-wrapper.md, and cite ADR 0103 in docs/security.md, the README row and the STATUS row. Check docs/adr/ on main for the next free number first.
- Anchors moved: `OrgSecret.kek_id` is now apps/api/src/choregos_api/db/models.py:702 (was :673), and docs/security.md '## Secrets' is now L235 (was L233). The secret_store.py anchors are unchanged (the file is untouched on main).
- Vocabulary (ADR 0093 Decision 1): a Python entry-point package (group `choregos.plugins`) is now called an 'extension', and 'plugin' means an ADR 0093 plugin package. Write 'an extension can supply a KMS or Vault-transit wrapper (set_secret_store, or a SealedDbSecretStore built with a wrapper)' in security.md and in the ADR. Never call it a plugin.
- New consumers of the store landed on main and must stay green. services/plugin_servers.py seals each plugin server's bearer token through `resolved_configuration`/`store_ref`/`get_secret_store` (ADR 0096). apps/api/tests/test_plugin_servers.py reads the sealed values, and apps/api/tests/test_outbound_proxy_relay.py uses FakeSecretStore. Add `apps/api/tests/test_plugin_servers.py apps/api/tests/test_outbound_proxy_relay.py` to the first pytest command. They use only the public store API, so they need no code change.

**Tests (each fails without the change).**

- apps/api/tests/test_secret_key_wrapper.py :: test_a_kms_wrapper_seals_without_the_env_key :: with CHOREGOS_SECRETS_KEKS deleted, SealedDbSecretStore(wrapper=FakeKms()) round-trips put then get_many, FakeKms.calls == ['wrap', 'unwrap'], and the row's kek_id starts with 'fake-kms:'; fails today
- apps/api/tests/test_secret_key_wrapper.py :: test_rows_sealed_by_the_env_keyring_still_open :: a value sealed with SealedDbSecretStore(keyring=k) opens with SealedDbSecretStore(wrapper=EnvKeyWrapper(k))
- apps/api/tests/test_secret_key_wrapper.py :: test_rewrap_moves_rows_to_the_wrapper :: rows sealed under the env key k1 rewrap to FakeKms and then open with it
- apps/api/tests/test_secret_store.py and test_connector_secrets.py (existing) :: green

**Acceptance.**

- [ ] Today's deployments behave identically, and a wrapper can replace the env keyring without changes in callers.
- [ ] make ci green. STATUS row: proves the seam with a fake KMS; ships no KMS integration (ADR 0103).

**Commands.**

```bash
uv run pytest apps/api/tests/test_secret_key_wrapper.py apps/api/tests/test_secret_store.py apps/api/tests/test_connector_secrets.py -q
uv run pytest apps/api/tests -q
uv run pytest tests/docs -q
uv run mypy packages/*/src apps/api/src apps/orchestrator/src tools
uv run python tools/check_english.py
make ci
```

**Do not.**

- Do not change the bytes, AAD or algorithm of existing envelopes.
- Do not add a KMS/Vault client dependency.
- Do not touch the chart.
- Do not edit docs/plan/STATUS.md or 08-backlog.yaml.

**Docs.** `docs/security.md '## Secrets'`, `docs/adr/0103-secret-keks-sit-behind-a-key-wrapper.md`, `docs/adr/README.md`

### S47-27 · test(orchestrator): the PostgreSQL mode migrates the schema and runs under the application role

**Wave** 2 · **Priority** P2 · **Size** M · **Depends on** `S47-22` · **Contract change** no

**PR title:** `test(orchestrator): the PostgreSQL mode migrates the schema and runs under the application role`

**Goal.** The orchestrator's PostgreSQL mode exercises what production runs: a schema built by the Alembic migrations, and a non-superuser role subject to RLS. Today it uses `create_all()` as the CI superuser, which bypasses RLS. Test-side unscoped sessions are translated to platform scope, as the API suite does, so the run reveals product defects rather than test-setup noise.

**Context.**

apps/orchestrator/tests/conftest.py:34-49 and :65 drop the public schema and run `create_all()` as the CI superuser (ci.yml:207 POSTGRES_USER=choregos).

The API PostgreSQL mode, in apps/api/tests/conftest.py:
- L41-53 monkeypatch `db_session.session_scope` so an unscoped call becomes `orgs='*'`, justified because product code never calls it unscoped.
- L160-178 `_preparer_postgres` creates the non-superuser role and `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS vector` as superuser.
- `_migrer` runs `command.upgrade(configuration(), 'heads')` from choregos_api.migrer.
- `_url_app` builds the application URL.

Under forced RLS an unscoped `session_scope()` sees no rows. apps/orchestrator/tests calls it unscoped 214 times, apps/orchestrator/src 0 times.

Test modules import session_scope inside their functions (`from choregos_api.db.session import session_scope`), so patching the module attribute reaches them. API test helpers are imported relatively (`from .catalogue_kit import ...`); orchestrator tests follow the same pattern.

This story runs after S47-22, which enables the mode in CI and may have touched the same conftest.

**Files.**

- Create: `apps/orchestrator/tests/pg_kit.py`, `apps/orchestrator/tests/test_postgres_mode_runs_under_rls.py`
- Modify: `apps/orchestrator/tests/conftest.py`

**Steps.**

1. apps/orchestrator/tests/pg_kit.py: copy into English the minimal helpers of apps/api/tests/conftest.py: create the application role if it is absent (NOSUPERUSER), grant USAGE/CREATE on schema public, `CREATE EXTENSION IF NOT EXISTS vector` as superuser (suppress the error when the image lacks it), run `command.upgrade(configuration(), 'heads')` from choregos_api.migrer, and build the application-role URL. Do not import across test packages.
2. conftest.py `setup`, in ON_POSTGRES mode: reset the schema, migrate with pg_kit, point CHOREGOS_DATABASE_URL at the application-role URL instead of calling `create_all()`, and monkeypatch `choregos_api.db.session.session_scope` so that an unscoped call becomes `orgs='*'`, with the comment of apps/api/tests/conftest.py:43-46 translated: product code never calls it unscoped, as the test below proves. The fixture's own seeding uses that platform scope.
3. Run the suite on PostgreSQL and fix test-setup issues. For each failure that reveals a product defect under RLS, open a `finding` issue (origin S47-27) and narrow the CI step from S47-22 with a named `-k` exclusion carrying the issue number. In that case also add .github/workflows/ci.yml to the PR, after checking that no other open story edits it.

**Recalibrated on main 3156d12 (overrides the text above).**

- apps/api/tests/conftest.py moved by +2 lines (a new `CHOREGOS_PLUGIN_SERVERS_REGISTRIES` default at L20-21). The unscoped-to-platform patch is now L42-55 (its comment L44-47). `_url_app` is now L139-149, `_administrer` L152-161, `_preparer_postgres` L164-180 and `_migrer` L183-187. The orchestrator conftest is unchanged (L34-49, L65).
- db/session.py on main (ADR 0090): `session_scope(orgs=[...])` with a LIST now goes through `limit_to_remembered_organisations` (L127-140). It reads `org_slug_aliases` under platform scope before narrowing. The signature of `session_scope(org_slug=None, *, orgs=None)` is unchanged. Have the test-side patch forward an explicit `org_slug`/`orgs` untouched (as the API patch does), so this alias path runs under RLS. A failure there is a product finding, not a setup issue.
- `command.upgrade(configuration(), 'heads')` now also applies the plugin stream's revisions (org_plugins, org_plugin_approvals, plugin_servers, org_slug_aliases, cost_ledger_org_level). There is still a single head (revision c9e1a3b5d7f8, 20261028_0900_plugin_servers.py), so test_the_suite_connects_as_a_role_that_is_not_superuser can compare alembic_version to that single head.

**Tests (each fails without the change).**

- apps/orchestrator/tests/test_postgres_mode_runs_under_rls.py :: test_the_suite_connects_as_a_role_that_is_not_superuser :: skipped unless ON_POSTGRES; `SELECT rolsuper FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = current_user` is false and alembic_version holds the head; fails today (superuser, no alembic_version)
- apps/orchestrator/tests/test_postgres_mode_runs_under_rls.py :: test_product_code_never_opens_an_unscoped_session :: an ast walk over apps/orchestrator/src finds no call to `session_scope` without arguments (the justification of the test-side translation); self-test on a synthetic file

**Acceptance.**

- [ ] In CI, the orchestrator suite on PostgreSQL runs against a migrated schema under a non-superuser role, and every exclusion is tracked by a finding.
- [ ] STATUS row: proves RLS-mode orchestration tests in CI and lists the findings opened.

**Commands.**


### S47-29 · refactor(api): split routers/agents.py into a package without changing a route

**Wave** 15 · **Priority** P2 · **Size** M · **Depends on** `S47-10`, `S45-15`, `S45-17` · **Contract change** no

**PR title:** `refactor(api): split routers/agents.py into a package without changing a route`

**Goal.** routers/agents.py (1,844 lines) becomes a package of cohesive modules of at most 600 lines each. Route order, operation ids, behaviour and every import path used elsewhere stay the same, and the layering ratchets keep covering the package.

**Context.**

routers/agents.py is the largest module of the API (1,844 lines, 88 SQL calls), registered as `agents.router` in main.py:339. After S47-10 its shared helpers live in services/agent_access.py and services/agent_views.py. FastAPI matches routes in registration order.

The S47-07 ratchets scan routers/**/*.py recursively. They exempt imports between modules of the same router package (`from ._router import router`, `from ._helpers import _x`), and forbid private imports across packages (CEILING 0 since S47-10). The organisation-lookup ratchet (CEILING 0) also scans recursively.

tools/check_english.py reads moved lines as added lines.

This story runs after the S45 rename of apps/api (symbolic dependency S45:apps-api-rename): use the names S45 left. Before splitting, grep `routers.agents` and `from .agents` across apps, packages and tests to list every imported name.

**Files.**

- Create: `apps/api/src/choregos_api/routers/agents/__init__.py`, `apps/api/src/choregos_api/routers/agents/_router.py`, `apps/api/src/choregos_api/routers/agents/(one module per route group, named by concern from the route prefixes: catalogue, versions, pins, credentials, lifecycle)`, `tests/architecture/fixtures/agents_routes.json`, `tests/architecture/test_agents_router_split.py`
- Modify: `apps/api/src/choregos_api/routers/agents.py`
- Delete: `apps/api/src/choregos_api/routers/agents.py (replaced by the package)`

**Steps.**

1. Before moving anything, capture the router's ordered list of (path, sorted methods, operation_id) into tests/architecture/fixtures/agents_routes.json.
2. Create the package:
   - `_router.py` defines `router = APIRouter(tags=['agents'])`.
   - Each submodule decorates that router.
   - `__init__.py` imports the submodules in exactly the captured order and re-exports `router` and every name other modules or tests import (for example `Slug`).
3. Move each route group, with the private helpers only it uses, into its submodule. Keep each file at 600 lines or fewer. Imports between modules of the package are allowed; imports of another router's private names are not (ratchet).
4. Every moved line is an added line for tools/check_english.py: rewrite docstrings and comments in English. Error strings stay byte-identical.
5. Run the API suite, tests/architecture (the ratchets included) and the replay suite.

**Integrator amendments (they override the text above).**

- Re-anchor every path and symbol of this story to the English names from the rename tables in `docs/upgrade-notes/0.21/` before starting (the story text was written against the names of 2026-10-27).

**Recalibrated on main 3156d12 (overrides the text above).**

- `agents.router` is now registered at apps/api/src/choregos_api/main.py:355 (was :339). routers/agents.py is unchanged on main (1,844 lines), and its importers are unchanged: routers/agent_realtime.py:52, agent_releases.py:34, agent_sessions.py:29, and apps/api/tests/test_agent_history.py:165 (`_pins`). The new services/plugin_installer.py:69 `from .agents import empreinte, erreurs_d_une_version` imports services/agents.py, not the router, so it is unaffected.

**Tests (each fails without the change).**

- tests/architecture/test_agents_router_split.py :: test_the_routes_keep_their_order_and_ids :: the router's (path, methods, operation_id) list equals the fixture
- tests/architecture/test_agents_router_split.py :: test_no_module_of_the_package_exceeds_600_lines :: fails today (agents.py has 1,844 lines)
- tests/architecture/test_agents_router_split.py :: test_the_public_names_still_import :: `from choregos_api.routers.agents import router, Slug` works
- apps/api/tests/test_layering_router_private_imports.py and test_one_organisation_lookup.py (S47-07/S47-10) :: green, scanning the package
- apps/api/tests (existing) :: green

**Acceptance.**

- [ ] Same routes in the same order, same behaviour, files of at most 600 lines, and the ratchets still cover the package.
- [ ] make ci green. STATUS row: a mechanical split, no behaviour change.

**Commands.**

```bash
uv run pytest tests/architecture apps/api/tests -q
uv run pytest tests/replay -q
uv run mypy packages/*/src apps/api/src apps/orchestrator/src tools
uv run python tools/check_english.py
make ci
```

**Do not.**

- Do not change any route path, operation id, status, message or dependency.
- Do not mix this split with renames or other refactors.
- Do not raise any ratchet ceiling.
- Do not run before the S45 rename of apps/api.

### S47-31 · refactor(api): split db/models.py into a package of domain modules with identical metadata

**Wave** 15 · **Priority** P2 · **Size** L · **Depends on** `S47-05`, `S47-14`, `S45-48`, `S45-13` · **Contract change** no

**PR title:** `refactor(api): split db/models.py into a package of domain modules with identical metadata`

**Goal.** db/models.py (1,560 lines) becomes a package of domain modules. Every class and public name stays importable from choregos_api.db.models, and the SQLAlchemy metadata (tables, columns, constraints, indexes) is identical, so Alembic sees no difference.

**Context.**

db/models.py is a hot spot (1,560 lines at b3a7893; S47-05 and S47-14 add a column and a model). It is imported by:
- the orchestrator (34 statements of `choregos_api.db.models`, counted by the S47-07 ratchet);
- the ontology plugin (choregos_ontology/service/actions.py:131, :281, :290);
- every API module;
- Alembic env and apps/api/tests/test_migrations.py.

SQLAlchemy relationships refer to classes by string, so `__init__` must import every submodule. check_english reads moved lines.

This story runs after the S45 rename of apps/api and after every S47 story that adds a model. The metadata fixture, not a class count, is the source of truth.

**Files.**

- Create: `apps/api/src/choregos_api/db/models/__init__.py`, `apps/api/src/choregos_api/db/models/(domain modules: identity, projects, work_items, runs, agents, knowledge, connectors, secrets, costs, releases, audit; each at most 400 lines)`, `tests/architecture/fixtures/model_metadata.json`, `tests/architecture/fixtures/model_public_names.json`, `tests/architecture/test_models_split.py`
- Modify: `apps/api/src/choregos_api/db/models.py`
- Delete: `apps/api/src/choregos_api/db/models.py (replaced by the package)`

**Steps.**

1. Before moving anything, dump a canonical description of `Base.metadata` into model_metadata.json: for each table sorted by name, its columns (name, type repr, nullable, default and server_default reprs), primary key, foreign keys, unique constraints and indexes. Also dump the sorted public namespace of the module (names not starting with '_') into model_public_names.json.
2. Create the package and move the classes by domain. Cross-module references use `from __future__ import annotations` plus TYPE_CHECKING imports. `__init__.py` imports every submodule and re-exports every name of model_public_names.json, with an `__all__`.
3. Rewrite the docstrings and comments of moved lines in English (check_english); keep table names, column names and defaults byte-identical.
4. Run the migrations tests, the API, orchestrator and ontology suites, and replay.

**Integrator amendments (they override the text above).**

### S47-32 · refactor(orchestrator): split workflows/interpreter.py while keeping every Temporal handler in place

**Wave** 5 · **Priority** P2 · **Size** L · **Depends on** `S47-23`, `S45-22` · **Contract change** no

**PR title:** `refactor(orchestrator): split workflows/interpreter.py while keeping every Temporal handler in place`

**Goal.** workflows/interpreter.py (1,650 lines) moves its undecorated helper methods into mixin modules, and its pure functions and constants into sibling modules. Every @workflow.run, signal, query and update handler, every workflow.patched id, every name imported or monkeypatched elsewhere stays where it is. All histories replay.

**Context.**

apps/orchestrator/src/choregos_orchestrator/workflows/interpreter.py has 1,650 lines.

Things that must not move or change:
- `@workflow.defn` class WorkflowInterpreter (sandboxed=False).
- The signals `inbound` (L295-297) and `control` (L323).
- The frozen patch ids at L76-90, pinned with an exact line format by tests/replay/test_english_names.py:33-50.
- The error type strings (L176-183).
- The names imported elsewhere, e.g. workflows/trial.py:18 `from .interpreter import DEFAULT_RETRY, POLL_RETRY`.
- The names tests patch: apps/orchestrator/tests/test_stage_config_refusal.py:126-133 monkeypatches `interpreter.workflow.patched` and reads `interpreter.STAGE_CONFIG_REFUSAL_ESCALATES`.

Other facts:
- Whether temporalio discovers handlers defined on base classes is unverified, so handlers stay in the class body.
- check_english reads moved lines.
- S47-23 makes every workflow class have a history before the split.
- This story runs after the S45 rename of apps/orchestrator (symbolic dependency S45:apps-orchestrator-rename), which edits the same file.

**Files.**

- Create: `apps/orchestrator/src/choregos_orchestrator/workflows/interpreter_parts/__init__.py`, `apps/orchestrator/src/choregos_orchestrator/workflows/interpreter_parts/(mixins by concern: stages, humans, trains, external waits; plus a pure-helpers module)`, `tests/architecture/test_interpreter_split.py`
- Modify: `apps/orchestrator/src/choregos_orchestrator/workflows/interpreter.py`

**Steps.**

1. List every decorated member of WorkflowInterpreter (run, signal, query, update) and every module-level name that other modules import (grep `from .interpreter import` and `workflows.interpreter`). Also list every name tests patch or read through the module: run `grep -rn "setattr(interpreter\|interpreter\." apps/orchestrator/tests tests`. All of these stay in interpreter.py, or are re-exported from it when they are read-only constants.
2. Move the undecorated helper methods into mixin classes under interpreter_parts/*, and make `class WorkflowInterpreter(<mixins>)` inherit them. Move the pure module-level functions and the non-patch constants into a helpers module, and re-export them from interpreter.py. Code that calls `workflow.patched` must keep calling it through a module the tests patch (keep it in interpreter.py or call `interpreter.workflow.patched` indirectly); check with test_stage_config_refusal.py.
3. Keep every `workflow.patched` constant line byte-identical in interpreter.py. Rewrite the docstrings and comments of moved lines in English. Run tests/replay and the orchestrator suite.

**Goal.** activities/stage.py (1,344 lines) keeps its activity functions with their registered names. Its helpers move to sibling modules (backend selection and refusals, MCP and tool refs, budget refusals) and are re-exported, so existing imports keep working. Every helper a test monkeypatches stays in stage.py together with its callers.

**Context.**

apps/orchestrator/src/choregos_orchestrator/activities/stage.py has 1,344 lines. A bare `@activity.defn` uses the function name as the Temporal name, so activity functions must not move or be renamed.

Helpers include:

- `_fixed_backend` (L1194-1197);

### S47-35 · feat(adapters): a GitLab CI adapter (pipeline status, job logs, trigger)

**Wave** 15 · **Priority** P2 · **Size** M · **Depends on** `S47-21`, `S45-29` · **Contract change** no

**PR title:** `feat(adapters): a GitLab CI adapter (pipeline status, job logs, trigger)`

**Goal.** A project whose code lives on GitLab can use `ci: gitlab`: pipeline status for a commit, job logs and a pipeline trigger. GitLab pipeline webhooks already parse into events. The type ships with its procedure, its docs export and an icon decision.

**Context.**

CiAdapter protocol (packages/adapters/src/choregos_adapters/base.py:97-102): `status_for(repo, sha) -> CiStatus`, `logs(run_ref, tail)`, `trigger(repo, ref, pipeline)` and `parse_event(headers, body)`. CiStatus is at packages/core/src/choregos_core/domain.py:190-194.

Registry: only `ci: tekton` ships. The GitLab tracker factory (__init__.py:1058-1068) builds `RestClient(cfg.get('base_url', 'https://gitlab.com'), headers={'PRIVATE-TOKEN': cfg['token']}, service='gitlab')` from packages/adapters/src/choregos_adapters/http/rest.py:22. Its spec (:258-264) declares no `egress`, so the hosts come from the URL-shaped fields and their defaults (registry.py:102-104).

GitLab pipeline events are parsed by tracker/gitlab_events.py `_pipeline` (L147) and routed by POST /webhooks/gitlab (routers/webhooks.py:422-444).

Other facts:
- Every type needs a procedure (tests/procedures/test_coverage.py). export_docs rewrites docs/procedures/README.md and, with --mocks, apps/web/src/mocks/procedures.json.
- A new registry type turns tests/web/test_connector_icon_fixture_is_current.py red until apps/web/tests/fixtures/connector-types.json is regenerated.
- apps/web/src/components/connector-icon.tsx:43-57 BRAND_OF_TYPE has 'gitlab-issues' but not 'gitlab'.
- The adapters registry is a hot spot, edited after S47-21.

**Files.**

- Create: `packages/adapters/src/choregos_adapters/ci/gitlab.py`, `packages/core/src/choregos_core/procedures/connector.ci.gitlab.md`, `docs/procedures/connector.ci.gitlab.md`, `packages/adapters/tests/test_gitlab_ci.py`
- Modify: `packages/adapters/src/choregos_adapters/__init__.py`, `apps/web/src/mocks/procedures.json`, `apps/web/tests/fixtures/connector-types.json`, `apps/web/src/components/connector-icon.tsx`, `apps/web/tests/connector-icon.test.ts`, `docs/procedures/README.md`

**Steps.**

1. ci/gitlab.py `class GitLabCi(client: RestClient, project: str)`:
   - status_for: GET /api/v4/projects/:id/pipelines?sha=<sha>&order_by=id&sort=desc; take the latest and map its status into CiStatus: success; failed -> failure; running; pending/created -> pending; canceled/skipped -> error; anything else -> unknown. GET its /jobs to fill `runs`.
   - logs: run_ref is '<job_id>'; GET /jobs/:id/trace and return the last `tail` lines.
   - trigger: POST /pipeline?ref=<ref>; return the pipeline id as a string.
   - parse_event: delegate pipeline payloads to gitlab_events.parse_gitlab_event('Pipeline Hook', ...).
   The project id is URL-encoded.
2. __init__.py: add the spec ('ci', 'gitlab'): display 'GitLab CI', config base_url (default https://gitlab.com) and project, secret field token, no explicit egress (the base_url host is inferred, as for gitlab-issues). Register a factory next to tekton that builds the RestClient exactly as the ('tracker', 'gitlab-issues') factory does.
3. connector-icon.tsx: add `gitlab: 'gitlab'` to BRAND_OF_TYPE (covers ci/gitlab, and scm/gitlab from S47-36). connector-icon.test.ts: assert `brand('ci', 'gitlab')` is 'gitlab'.
4. Write the procedure, following connector.ci.tekton.md and connector.tracker.gitlab-issues.md. Run `uv run python tools/procedures/export_docs.py` and `uv run python tools/procedures/export_docs.py --mocks apps/web/src/mocks/procedures.json`. Regenerate apps/web/tests/fixtures/connector-types.json from `connector_types()`.

**Integrator amendments (they override the text above).**

In routers/webhooks.py, the GitLab route is now L426-447 (was 422-444). registry.py:102-104 (egress) and connector-icon.tsx:43-57 (BRAND_OF_TYPE) are unchanged. connector-types.json, procedures.json and docs/procedures/README.md already contain the plugin stream's tracker/plugin entries: regenerate them, never hand-merge. connector-icon.test.ts gained a tracker/plugin lucide assertion (L46) on main; keep it.

### S47-36 · feat(adapters): a GitLab SCM adapter with a one-day project access token per run

**Wave** 16 · **Priority** P2 · **Size** L · **Depends on** `S47-35`, `S47-26` · **Contract change** no

**PR title:** `feat(adapters): a GitLab SCM adapter with a one-day project access token per run`

**Goal.** A project whose code lives on GitLab can use `scm: gitlab`: branches, commits, merge requests, comments, reviewers, auto-merge when the pipeline succeeds, and compare. Each run gets a token scoped to write_repository that expires within a day; expired run tokens are cleaned up so their bot users do not pile up. The token model and the tier requirement are recorded in an ADR.

**Context.**

ScmAdapter protocol (packages/adapters/src/choregos_adapters/base.py:79-94) has 12 methods: mint_token, ensure_branch, branch_exists, commit_files, open_pr, update_pr, comment_pr, get_pr, request_review, enqueue_merge, compare and parse_webhook. Domain types: PrRef and PrState (domain.py:102-133), DiffSummary (:170).

Registry: only `scm: github` ships. GitLab merge-request events are parsed by gitlab_events.py `_merge_request` (L121).

GitLab facts:
- Project access tokens expire on a DATE (day granularity).
- On GitLab.com they need the Premium or Ultimate tier.
- Each token creates a bot user.
- POST /repository/branches answers 400 'Branch already exists' (not 409).
- `merge_when_pipeline_succeeds` is deprecated in favour of `auto_merge` on recent GitLab.

The runner must never hold a broad token (AGENTS.md). Revocation at run end is out of reach because the protocol has no revoke.

Build the client as the GitLab tracker factory does (RestClient with PRIVATE-TOKEN). S47-35 added the `gitlab` brand icon. The connector-types fixture, the procedures index and mocks change again here.

**Files.**

- Create: `packages/adapters/src/choregos_adapters/scm/gitlab.py`, `packages/core/src/choregos_core/procedures/connector.scm.gitlab.md`, `docs/procedures/connector.scm.gitlab.md`, `packages/adapters/tests/test_gitlab_scm.py`, `docs/adr/0104-gitlab-runs-get-a-one-day-project-access-token.md`
- Modify: `packages/adapters/src/choregos_adapters/__init__.py`, `apps/web/src/mocks/procedures.json`, `apps/web/tests/fixtures/connector-types.json`, `docs/procedures/README.md`, `docs/adr/README.md`

**Steps.**

1. scm/gitlab.py `class GitLabScm(client: RestClient, project: str, default_branch: str = 'main')` implements the 12 methods with GitLab REST v4:
   - mint_token: first, best effort, list the project's access tokens (GET /projects/:id/access_tokens), and DELETE those whose name starts with 'choregos-run-' and whose expires_at is in the past (at most 20 per call; log and ignore errors). Then POST /projects/:id/access_tokens with name `choregos-run-<short id>`, scopes ['write_repository'], access_level 30 (Developer) and expires_at = tomorrow (UTC date); return the token.
   - ensure_branch: POST /repository/branches; a 400 whose message contains 'already exists' means it exists.
   - branch_exists: GET /repository/branches/:name.
   - commit_files: POST /repository/commits with create/update actions; returns the sha.
   - open_pr: POST /merge_requests (draft through the 'Draft: ' title prefix); returns a PrRef whose number is the iid.
   - update_pr: PUT /merge_requests/:iid.
   - comment_pr: POST /merge_requests/:iid/notes.
   - get_pr: GET /merge_requests/:iid and its pipelines, to build PrState.
   - request_review: look up reviewers with GET /users?username=, then PUT reviewer_ids.
   - enqueue_merge: PUT /merge_requests/:iid/merge with BOTH `auto_merge=true` and `merge_when_pipeline_succeeds=true` (recent GitLab reads the first, older the second).
   - compare: GET /repository/compare?from&to, mapped into DiffSummary.
   - parse_webhook: delegate to gitlab_events.
2. __init__.py: add the spec ('scm', 'gitlab'): base_url (default https://gitlab.com), project and default_branch; secret field token. Register it next to github, building the RestClient as the gitlab-issues factory does.
3. Write the procedure. It states that minting needs Maintainer and the `api` scope, that GitLab.com needs Premium or Ultimate for project access tokens, the one-day expiry, and the cleanup of expired run tokens. Run export_docs (docs and --mocks) and regenerate apps/web/tests/fixtures/connector-types.json.
4. Write docs/adr/0104-gitlab-runs-get-a-one-day-project-access-token.md (accepted, today):
   - The model: one token per run, write_repository only, expiring the next day.
   - Why not cache one token per (project, day): it would need storing a live secret.
   - Why not the configured token, nor a group deploy token: too broad.
   - The tier requirement.
   - Residual risk: no revoke until expiry; expired tokens are cleaned at the next mint. Open a finding.
   Append the README row.

**Integrator amendments (they override the text above).**

- Re-anchor every path and symbol of this story to the English names from the rename tables in `docs/upgrade-notes/0.21/` before starting (the story text was written against the names of 2026-10-27).

**Recalibrated on main 3156d12 (overrides the text above).**

- ADR number: under the plan's renumbering this hardening ADR is 0104 (was 0100). Create docs/adr/0104-gitlab-runs-get-a-one-day-project-access-token.md and cite ADR 0104 in the README row and the procedure. Check docs/adr/ on main for the next free number first. Anchors: the ('scm','github') spec is now __init__.py L293 and its registration L1113. Build the RestClient as the gitlab-issues factory at L1102-1112 does.

**Tests (each fails without the change).**

- packages/adapters/tests/test_gitlab_scm.py :: test_mint_token_asks_for_write_repository_until_tomorrow :: the respx-recorded POST /access_tokens body has scopes ['write_repository'] and expires_at = tomorrow; fails today (no module)
- packages/adapters/tests/test_gitlab_scm.py :: test_mint_token_cleans_expired_run_tokens :: an expired 'choregos-run-old' token is DELETEd, while an unexpired one and a non-choregos one are kept

- packages/adapters/tests/test_gitlab_scm.py :: test_open_pr_then_auto_merge :: open_pr returns PrRef(number=iid), and enqueue_merge sends auto_merge=true and merge_when_pipeline_succeeds=true
- packages/adapters/tests/test_gitlab_scm.py :: test_commit_files_sends_create_and_update_actions

- packages/adapters/tests/test_gitlab_scm.py :: test_the_type_is_registered_with_a_secret_token
- tests/procedures/test_coverage.py, tests/web/test_connector_icon_fixture_is_current.py and tests/docs/test_index_des_adr.py (existing) :: green

**Acceptance.**

- [ ] make ci and make web-ci green. STATUS row: proves the adapter against respx mocks. Does not prove it against gitlab.com, does not revoke tokens at run end (finding), and does not prove that GitLab deletes the bot users of revoked tokens.

```bash
make web-ci
uv run python tools/check_english.py

```

- Never hand the configured (Maintainer, api) token to a runner.

### S47-37 · docs(deployment): an 'Upgrading to 0.21' section collecting the operator-visible changes (integrator)

**Wave** 13 · **Priority** P1 · **Size** S · **Depends on** `S47-01`, `S47-02`, `S47-03`, `S47-04`, `S47-11`, `S47-12`, `S47-13`, `S47-14`, `S47-20`, `S47-38`, `S46-09`, `S46-12` · **Contract change** no

**PR title:** `docs(deployment): an 'Upgrading to 0.21' section collecting the operator-visible changes (integrator)`

**Goal.** An operator upgrading from 0.20.x reads, in one place, every change that can stop a start, fail a render or change behaviour, with the action to take. This is an integrator story: it collects the 'Release note' lines the stories wrote in their PR bodies.

**Context.**

The repository has no CHANGELOG and no upgrade section (no '# ...Upgrad...' heading in docs/*.md). Version 0.20.5 is in pyproject.toml:3 and charts/choregos/Chart.yaml:7.

The breaking or visible changes:
- S47-01: CHOREGOS_SESSION_SECRET of at least 32 bytes on deployed instances, checked by the API only; CHOREGOS_SESSION_SECRET_PREVIOUS for rotation; an extraEnv duplicate fails the render.
- S47-02: trusted proxies default to the private ranges; on-prem narrowing.
- S47-03: the chart renders CHOREGOS_CORS_ORIGINS; a value in extraEnv must move to choregos-api.corsOrigins.
- S47-04: discovery is mandatory and its issuer must match; explicit endpoints; email_verified.
- S47-05: memberships provenance; roles withdrawn before the upgrade are not revoked.
- S47-11: unenforced governance keys are refused; advanced template v3.
- S47-12: POST/MCP catalogue tools need read_only.
- S47-13: unsigned GitHub/Tekton webhooks are refused on https dev; tracker commands are answered.
- S47-14: pause/resume/stop from signed GitHub comments of linked ids.
- S47-20: the project allowlist is enforced.
- S47-38: tracker card moves no longer move items.

docs/deployment.md headings: '## Operating it' at about L1080.

**Files.**

- Create: `tests/docs/test_upgrade_notes.py`

### S47-38 · fix(api): a tracker card move no longer moves a work item

**Wave** 12 · **Priority** P1 · **Size** S · **Depends on** `S47-14` · **Contract change** no

**PR title:** `fix(api): a tracker card move no longer moves a work item`

**Goal.** A status change on a tracker board (GitHub Projects v2, Jira) is no longer delivered to the interpreter, which today moves a parked work item to whatever state's tracker.status matches, with no RBAC. This closes the same trust gap ADR 0102 closed for comments. The event is logged, the interpreter code path stays for replay, and the decision is recorded in ADR 0102.

**Context.**

apps/orchestrator/src/choregos_orchestrator/workflows/interpreter.py:1567-1572: for an inbox event of type 'tracker.item.moved', the interpreter returns the first state whose `tracker.status` equals `payload.to_status`.

Where the event comes from:
- packages/adapters/src/choregos_adapters/tracker/github_events.py:136-152 `_projects_v2_item` (the payload carries `sender.login` only as `actor`);
- jira_events.py:76-95 (status changelog);
- the fake tracker (fakes/tracker.py:64).

Other facts:
- No test in apps/ exercises the path, and no doc describes it.
- Choregos's own mirror (activities/tracker.py `mirror_state` -> `set_state`) moves cards, and its echoes come back as ITEM_MOVED: they must stay silent (no comment, no audit row).
- routers/webhooks.py `_dispatch` (after S47-13/S47-14) delivers every other event through `deliver_inbound`.
- The GitHub projects_v2_item payload used by packages/adapters/tests/test_github_tracker.py:446-458 routes to a project through `repository.full_name` and `work_item_key`.
- The Jira webhook tests in apps/api/tests show the shared-secret header to send.

The API stops sending; no workflow change, no replay needed.

**Files.**

- Create: `apps/api/tests/test_tracker_moves_are_not_decisions.py`
- Modify: `apps/api/src/choregos_api/routers/webhooks.py`, `docs/usage.md`, `docs/adr/0102-a-tracker-comment-carries-no-decision.md`

**Steps.**

1. webhooks.py `_dispatch`: right before the HUMAN_DECISION branch (S47-13), add `if event.type == InboundEventType.ITEM_MOVED: logger.info('tracker move not applied: a card move is not a decision (ADR 0102)', project=project.slug, work_item=key, to_status=event.payload.get('to_status')); continue`. It is not counted in `delivered`, writes no audit row and posts no comment (mirror echoes).
2. ADR 0102: add '## Card moves'. A board status change is not a decision and is never delivered to the workflow; Choregos's own moves come back as echoes and are ignored the same way; a person moves a work item from the console. The interpreter branch remains for histories recorded before this change.
3. docs/usage.md '## 5. Let a ticket run': one paragraph. Moving a card on the board does not move the work item; the board follows Choregos, not the reverse. Write a 'Release note' line in the PR body.

**Recalibrated on main 3156d12 (overrides the text above).**

- ADR number: the ADR that S47-13 creates ('a tracker comment carries no decision', hardening 0098) is 0102 under the plan's renumbering (0098 becomes the English ADR). Edit docs/adr/0102-a-tracker-comment-carries-no-decision.md, not 0098-*, and write '(ADR 0102)' in the log message and in docs/usage.md.
- routers/webhooks.py `_dispatch` (L104-164 on main) now has the signature `_dispatch(session, events, project_of: Project | None = None)`. The plugin-tracker route `POST /webhooks/plugins/{org}/{plugin}/{server}` (L517-590, S45-07) also calls it, at L584. Keep the parameter. The ITEM_MOVED skip placed in `_dispatch` covers that route too. Plugin tracker profiles can only emit tracker.item.created/updated/commented/closed (packages/contracts/schemas/plugin-tracker-profile.schema.json L650-656), so no plugin test is needed.
- docs/usage.md '## 5. Let a ticket run' is now at L2254. The interpreter (L1569), github_events.py `_projects_v2_item` (L136-152), jira_events.py (L88) and fakes/tracker.py:64 anchors are unchanged, and the test_github_tracker.py payload is still at L446-458.

**Tests (each fails without the change).**

- apps/api/tests/test_tracker_moves_are_not_decisions.py :: test_a_github_card_move_is_not_signalled :: with the interpreter started for varga/billing-api#12, POST /api/v1/webhooks/github (X-GitHub-Event: projects_v2_item, payload as test_github_tracker.py:446-458) answers 202, and FakeTemporal.signals holds no 'inbound' whose payload type is tracker.item.moved. Today it is signalled
- apps/api/tests/test_tracker_moves_are_not_decisions.py :: test_a_jira_status_change_is_not_signalled :: a Jira issue_updated delivery with a status changelog item sends no tracker.item.moved signal
- apps/api/tests/test_tracker_moves_are_not_decisions.py :: test_other_events_still_reach_the_interpreter :: a GitHub issues 'labeled' delivery for the same item still sends its inbound signal

**Acceptance.**

- [ ] No tracker card move reaches a workflow; other tracker events still do.
- [ ] make ci green. STATUS row: proves the filtering by tests. Removes an untested, undocumented path; does not offer moves from linked identities. 'Release note' line in the PR body.

```bash
uv run pytest tests/replay -q
```

## 6. Verification (end to end)

Per story: the story's own commands, then `make ci` (+ `make web-ci`, `make charts-lint`,
`make policies-test`, the replay suite where the story says so). Per wave: the integrator
commit of §1.2 step 6. At the milestones below, run these checks and record the result in
STATUS:

| When | Check | Proves |
|:--|:--|:--|
| after wave 3 | `uv run pytest apps/api/tests/test_securite_authentification.py -q` (renamed later) and a manual sign-in on `docker compose up` with a Keycloak user whose email is changed to another user's, unverified | SEC-1…SEC-8 closed: forged sessions, XFF rotation, cross-origin cookie POST, unverified email takeover all refused |
| after wave 5 | `uv run pytest apps/orchestrator/tests -q` with `CHOREGOS_TEST_ORCH_ON_POSTGRES=1`; `uv run pytest tests/replay -q` | the orchestrator on PostgreSQL; 13/13 workflows have a replayed history |
| after wave 10 | `uv run pytest tests/e2e/test_mcp_gateway.py -q` (S46-11): an agent run reads through the fake gateway and a governed write waits for approval, then reaches the upstream server once; `CHOREGOS_FAKES=1 docker compose --profile mcp-gateway up` and the console's MCP gateway card **Test** button | the gateway path end to end, governance unchanged, no runner ever sees the gateway URL |
| nightly, once a real gateway is set | `CHOREGOS_LIVE_MCP_GATEWAY_URL=… CHOREGOS_LIVE_MCP_GATEWAY_SERVER=… uv run pytest tests/live/test_mcp_gateway_live.py -m live -rs` (S46-16) | a real gateway product speaks to Choregos through public interfaces only |
| after wave 14 (0.21.0) | `make ci`, `make web-ci`, `make e2e`, `make demo`, `docker compose up` + `tools/local_install_check.py`; upgrade a 0.20.5 database with `python -m choregos_api.migrer` and the old env var names | the release works for an existing install through its shims |
| after wave 26 | `uv run python tools/check_english.py --all --report` (strict at zero since S45-47) reports **0** prose, identifier and path findings; `git ls-files docs/fr` is empty; `grep -rl "# Moved" docs/adr` returns nothing | the project is fully English; archives deleted |
| after wave 27 | `make ci`; the plugin API, env vars and contract fields of the alias table answer with their English names only | external aliases retired on schedule |

## 7. Decisions taken, defaults chosen, and what this plan does not cover

**Owner decisions (2026-10-27).** MCP gateway = another product, consumed through public
interfaces (ADR 0097). English: everything, with shims; French archives deleted. Tracker: no
decision from a comment; pause/resume/stop from GitHub accounts an org admin linked; card moves
no longer move work items. Ecosystem: SMTP notifier (P1), GitLab CI and SCM (P2) included.
Allowed agent backends enforced at stage start (with an upgrade note). External aliases live one
release (removed in 0.22.0).

**Defaults chosen by the plan (the integrator may change them before dispatching the story).**

- Grafana dashboard uids stay frozen (no alias exists); the four history files whose stem is a
  recorded workflow id stay frozen; one deliberately French eval ticket
  (`py-rounding-bug-fr.yaml`) is kept for the "English answer to a French ticket" case.
- Persisted French JSON (event payload `cle`, action params `ontologie`, results
  `attente`/`preuves`, audit keys `jeton`/`ecriture`/`avant`/`apres`, issue `refus`) is read
  under both names forever and never rewritten; writers switch to English in S45-39.
- `?jeton=` on the Tekton sink is an external alias removed in 0.22.0 (sink URLs live outside
  the repo: the upgrade note says so). Kyverno policy/rule renames ship with upgrade notes; the
  operator updates its PolicyExceptions. Log event names are renamed with upgrade-note rows.
- S47-02 trusts private address ranges as proxies by default (right for cloud ingress); on-prem
  installs whose clients have private addresses must narrow it (upgrade note).
- S47-14 links a member's GitHub numeric id on an org admin's word (no OAuth proof); only
  HMAC-verified GitHub deliveries count. Jira display names and GitLab shared-secret deliveries
  are not identities.
- MCP gateway: `DELETE` refused while `mcp-gateway` connectors exist; `off` keeps them but
  refuses calls; key rotation publishes the new key and keeps the previous one; the assertion
  carries the initiator's and approvers' emails; agent sessions carry `project:<slug>` as
  subject; tool annotations served through the gateway get the same trust as a direct server's
  (born forbidden, opened by an admin).
- Before S47-01 merges, someone checks that every deployed `CHOREGOS_SESSION_SECRET` (e.g. the
  dev tenant's) is at least 32 bytes, or that API will refuse to start.

**Not covered (file a `finding` issue each, `origin: plan-2026-10-27`).**

- Server-side offboarding of API tokens and sessions when the IdP removes a user (no SCIM in the
  community edition); S47-05 revokes roles at the next sign-in only.
- Backup monitoring, the S3 WAL key in clear text, HA with 3-node Temporal and 3-instance CNPG,
  the 15 unplayed runbooks (operator repositories, SEC-11).
- Extracting a `choregos-persistence` package so the orchestrator stops depending on
  `choregos-api` (ARC-3): S47-07 only freezes the coupling; a `plugin_sdk` facade for the
  ontology plugin (ARC-5).
- RFC 9728 protected-resource discovery and RFC 7523 `private_key_jwt` for the gateway's OAuth;
  an organisation-chosen minimal assertion claim set; a migration of existing direct `mcp`
  connectors behind the gateway that keeps their operation policies.
- The dead `reviewers` branch of `apps/orchestrator/.../activities/scm.py:65-67` (FUN-6), the
  766 function-local imports and 2,874 `Any` (TEC-2), the web bundle at its budget and the
  3,464-line mock file (TEC-3), agent evals that check instructions rather than behaviour
  (TST-6), a non-Argo CD adapter, A2A on-behalf-of (#756), hosted agents in a real cluster, the
  data-platform stream S14.
